特平疾步蹿上陡峭的木楼梯，发现自己置身另一条走廊，光线颇亮，两头各有一扇门。他走了左手那扇，一头冲了过去，心里正盘算怎样才能取回帽子和大衣，还有玛丽不知怎么样了。门很容易就开了，他仓促间向前跨了两步。门在身后荡回关上，他陷进一团漆黑，便回身想再把门打开，好借点光。可门怎么也开不开了。随着那扇门的阖拢，他整个人从人世上干干净净地走了出去。

===Original===
At first he was angry, and presently, when he realised his situation, a little alarmed. The place seemed to be small, it was utterly dark, and as stuffy as the inside of a safe. His chief thought at the moment was that it would never do for him to be caught in a raid on a dance-club, for his true name might come out and the harm which Archie’s foolish tongue had wrought might be thereby aggravated. But soon he saw that he had stepped out of one kind of danger into what was probably a worse. He was locked in an infernal cupboard in a house which he knew to have the most unholy connections.

===Chinese===
起初他只是恼火，待到弄清自己的处境，便有几分惊慌了。这地方似乎很小，黑得彻彻底底，闷得像保险柜的内部。他当时主要想的是：绝不能在突查舞厅的行动里被拿住，因为他的真名可能因此曝光，阿奇那张闯祸的嘴已经惹下的害处，会由此变本加厉。但他很快明白，自己是从一种危险里迈了出去，一脚踩进的没准更糟。他被锁在一间地狱般的小黑屋里，而这座宅子，他知道，有着最邪门的瓜葛。

===Original===
He started to grope around, and found that the place was larger than he thought. The walls were bare, the floor seemed to be of naked boards, and there was not a stick of furniture anywhere, nor, so far as he could see, any window. He could not discover the door he had entered by, which on the inside must have been finished dead level with the walls. Presently he found that his breathing was difficult, and that almost put him in a panic, for the dread of suffocation had always been for him the private funk from which the bravest man is not free. To breathe was like having his face tightly jammed against a pillow. He made an effort and controlled himself, for he realised that if he let himself become hysterical he would only suffocate the faster.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他开始四下摸索，发现这地方比想的要大。墙壁光秃秃的，地面像是裸木板，四下没有一件家具，就他所能看清的，也没有一扇窗。他找不着进来时的那扇门——门在里面那一面，想必做得同墙面齐平，分毫不差。不一会儿他觉得呼吸艰难起来，这几乎叫他恐慌，因为对窒息的恐惧，向来是他私人独有的怯处——再勇敢的人也免不了各有一怕。呼吸就像脸被死死按在枕头上。他竭力按捺住自己，因为他明白，若是任自己歇斯底里起来，只会窒息得更快。……

===Original===
Then he declares that he felt a hand pressing on his mouth.﻿ ﻿… It must have been imagination, for he admits that the place was empty, but all the same the hand came again and again﻿—a large soft hand smelling of roses. His nerves began to scream, and his legs to give under him. The roses came down on him in a cloud, and that horrible flabby hand, as big as a hill, seemed to smother him. He tried to move, to get away from it, and before he knew he found himself on his knees. He struggled to get up, but the hand was on him, flattening him out, and that intolerable sweet sickly odour swathed him in its nauseous folds.﻿ ﻿… And then he lost consciousness.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
接着他一口咬定，他感到一只手压在他的嘴上。……那想必是幻觉，因为他承认那地方空无一人，可那只手还是一次又一次地压下来——一只肥大的、软绵绵的手，带着玫瑰的气味。他的神经开始尖叫，双腿开始发软。玫瑰花香像一团乌云罩头压下，那只可怕的、松软的手大得像一座山，仿佛要把他捂死。他挣扎着想动、想躲，不知不觉间已跪倒在地。他挣扎着想爬起来，那只手却压着他，把他摁得扁扁的，那股难以忍受的甜腻气味，用它令人作呕的褶层把他层层裹住。……随后他便失去了知觉。……

===Original===
How long he was senseless he doesn’t know, but he thinks it must have been a good many hours. When he came to he was no longer in the cupboard. He was lying on what seemed to be a couch in a room which felt spacious, for he could breathe freely, but it was still as black as the nether pit. He had a blinding headache, and felt rather sick and as silly as an owl. He couldn’t remember how he had come there, but as his hand fell on his shirtfront, and he realised he was in dress clothes, he recollected Archie’s cry. That was the last clear thing in his head, but it steadied him, for it reminded him how grave was his danger. He has told me that at first he was half stifled with panic, for he was feeling abominably weak; but he had just enough reason left in him to let him take a pull on his nerves. “You must be a man,” he repeated to himself. “Even if you have stumbled into hell, you must be a man.”

===Chinese===
他昏了多久自己也不知道，但想必有好多个钟头。醒过来时，他已不在那间小黑屋里。他躺在一张像是躺椅的东西上，所在的房间摸着宽敞——因为他呼吸畅快了——却依旧黑得如同九幽地府。他头痛欲裂，直想呕吐，呆得像只猫头鹰。他记不起自己是怎么到这儿来的，可当他的手落在衬衫前襟上、发觉自己还穿着晚礼服时，他想起了阿奇的那一声喊。这是他脑中最后一件清楚的事，倒是它让他定了神，因为它提醒了他，他的处境何等凶险。他告诉过我，起初他被恐慌憋得半死，因为他虚弱得厉害；但他脑壳里剩下的那点理智，还够他勒住自己的神经。“你必须做个男子汉，”他一遍遍对自己说。“哪怕你一脚踏进了地狱，你也必须做个男子汉。”

===Original===
Then a voice spoke out of the darkness, and at the sound of it most of his fright disappeared. It was no voice that he knew, but a pleasant voice, and it spoke to him in French. Not ordinary French, you understand, but the French of his native valley in the South, with the soft slurring patois of his home. It seemed to drive away his headache and nausea, and to soothe every jangled nerve, but it made him weaker. Of that he has no doubt. This friendly voice was making him a child again.

===Chinese===
随后黑暗里响起一个声音，一听见它，他的恐惧便去了大半。那不是他识得的声音，却是个悦耳的声音，说的是法语。你要明白，不是寻常的法语，而是他南方故乡村谷的法语，带着他家乡那种温软含混的土音。它仿佛驱散了他的头痛和恶心，抚平了每一根错乱的神经，却也使他更虚弱了。这一点他毫不怀疑。这个友善的声音，正在把他重新变回一个孩子。

===Original===
His memory of what it said is hopelessly vague. He thinks that it reminded him of the life of his boyhood﻿—the old château high in a fold of the limestone hills, the feathery chestnuts in the valley bottom, the clear pools where the big trout lived, the snowy winters when the wolves came out of the forests to the farmyard doors, the hot summers when the roads were blinding white and the turf on the downs grew as yellow as corn. The memory of it was all jumbled, and whatever the voice said its effect was more like music than spoken words. It smoothed out the creases in his soul, but it stole also the manhood from him. He was becoming limp and docile and passive like a weak child.

===Chinese===
他对那声音说了什么，记忆已糊成一团。他想，那是把他带回了少年时代的生活——古老的城堡（château）高踞在石灰岩山丘的一道褶皱里，谷底是羽毛般蓬松的栗树，清澈的水潭里栖着大鳟鱼，多雪的冬天狼群走出森林来到农家院门口，炎热的夏天道路白得晃眼、丘原上的草皮黄得像熟麦。这些记忆乱作一片，而不管那声音说了什么，它的效果与其说是言语，不如说更像音乐。它抚平了他灵魂上的褶皱，却也把他身上的男子气偷走了。他正变得瘫软、驯顺、听人摆布，像个虚弱的孩子。

===Original===
The voice stopped and he felt a powerful inclination to sleep. Then suddenly, between sleeping and waking, he became aware of a light, a star which glowed ahead of him in the darkness. It waxed and then waned, and held his eyes like a vice. At the back of his head he knew that there was some devilry in the business, that it was something which he ought to resist, but for the life of him he could not remember why.

===Chinese===
声音停了，他感到一阵不可抗拒的睡意。忽然，在半睡半醒之间，他觉察到一点光，一颗星，在他前方的黑暗里发亮。它盈了又亏，像老虎钳一样钳住他的眼睛。他脑子深处知道这事情里藏着邪祟，知道这是他应当抵抗的东西，可他怎么也想不起来为什么要抵抗。

===Original===
The light broadened till it was like the circle which a magic-lantern makes on a screen. Into the air there crept a strange scent﻿—not the sickly smell of roses, but a hard pungent smell which tantalised him with its familiarity. Where had he met it before?﻿ ﻿… Slowly out of it there seemed to shape a whole world of memories.

===Chinese===
光晕扩大开来，直到像幻灯机打在幕布上的那一圈亮。空气里潜入一股奇异的气味——不是玫瑰那股甜腻味，而是一种硬扎的、辛辣的气味，那股熟稔劲儿直撩拨他。他从前在哪儿闻到过？……慢慢地，从这气味里，仿佛塑出了一整个记忆的世界。

===Original===
Now Turpin before the War had put in some years’ service in Africa with the Armée Coloniale as a lieutenant of Spahis, and had gone with various engineering and military expeditions south of the Algerian frontier into the desert. He used to rave to me about the glories of those lost days, that first youth of a man which does not return.﻿ ﻿… This smell was the desert, that unforgettable, untameable thing which stretches from the Mediterranean to the Central African forests, the place where, in the days when it was sea, Ulysses wandered, and where the magic of Circe and Calypso for all the world knows may still linger.

===Chinese===
要知道，战前特平曾在非洲的法国殖民军（Armée Coloniale）里当过几年斯帕希骑兵（Spahis）的中尉，随各种工程和军事勘察队越过阿尔及利亚边境深入沙漠。他从前常对我如痴如醉地讲那些逝去岁月的荣光——人一生头一轮的青春，去了便不再回来。……这气味就是沙漠，那件难忘的、驯不服的东西，从地中海一直铺到中部非洲的森林；那地方，在它还是海洋的年代，尤利西斯曾在那里漂泊，而喀耳刻（Circe）和卡吕普索（Calypso）的魔法，普天之下谁都知道，说不定至今还在那里流连。

===Original===
In the moon of light a face appeared, a face so strongly lit up that every grim and subtle line of it was magnified. It was an Eastern face, a lean high-boned Arab face, with the eyes set in a strange slant. He had never seen it before, but he had met something like it when he had dabbled in the crude magic of the sands, the bubbling pot, and the green herb fire. At first it was only a face, half averted, and then it seemed to move so that the eyes appeared, like lights suddenly turned on at night as one looks from without at a dark house.

===Chinese===
在那一轮光月亮里，浮现出一张脸，一张被照得极强的脸，每一条狰狞而精微的纹路都被放大了。那是一张东方人的脸，一张瘦削、颧骨高耸的阿拉伯人的脸，两只眼睛生在一个古怪的斜度上。他从未见过这张脸，但当年他沾手过沙地上的粗野巫术——翻泡的锅、青草燃起的火——那时他见识过与此相类的东西。起初那只是半张侧转的脸，随后它仿佛转动起来，那双眼睛出现了，就像夜里人从外面望向一栋黑屋子时，屋里突然亮起的灯。

===Original===
He felt in every bone a thing he had almost forgotten, the spell and the terror of the desert. It was a cruel and inhuman face, hiding God knows what of ancient horror and sin, but wise as the Sphinx and eternal as the rocks. As he stared at it the eyes seemed to master and envelop him, and, as he put it, suck the soul out of him.

===Chinese===
他浑身的骨头里感到一件他几乎忘却了的东西——沙漠的魔咒和恐怖。那是一张残忍的、非人的脸，天知道藏着多少古老的恐怖与罪孽，却像斯芬克斯（Sphinx）一样智慧，像岩石一样永恒。他盯着它看时，那双眼睛仿佛镇住了他、裹住了他，用他的话说，把他的魂从他身体里吸了出去。

===Original===
You see he had never been told about Kharáma. That was the one mistake Mary made, and a very natural one, for it was not likely that he and the Indian would foregather. So he had nothing in his poor muddled head to help him to combat this mastering presence. He didn’t try. He said he felt himself sinking into a delicious lethargy, like the coma which overtakes a man who is being frozen to death.

===Chinese===
你要知道，从来没有人跟他提起过卡拉玛（Kharáma）。那是玛丽犯下的唯一一个错，也是极自然的一个错，因为他同那个印度人凑到一块儿的可能本就不大。于是他那颗可怜的、乱作一团的脑袋里，没有任何东西能帮他对抗这个摄人的存在。他也没有试。他说，他感到自己正沉入一种惬意的倦怠，像人被冻僵时漫上来的那种昏迷。

===Original===
I could get very little out of Turpin about what happened next. The face spoke to him, but whether in French or some African tongue he didn’t know﻿—French, he thought﻿—certainly not English. I gather that, while the eyes and the features were to the last degree awe-inspiring, the voice was, if anything, friendly. It told him that he was in instant danger, and that the only hope lay in utter impassivity. If he attempted to exercise his own will, he was doomed, and there was sufficient indication of what that doom meant to shake his lethargy into spasms of childish fear. “Your body is too feeble to move,” said the voice, “for Allah has laid His hand on it.” Sure enough Turpin realised that he hadn’t the strength of a kitten. “You have surrendered your will to Allah till He restores it to you.” That also was true, for Turpin knew he could not summon the energy to brush his hair, unless he was ordered to. “You will be safe,” said the voice, “so long as you sleep. You will sleep till I bid you awake.”

===Chinese===
接下来发生了什么，我几乎没能从特平嘴里掏出多少。那张脸对他说话了，但说的是法语还是某种非洲话，他不知道——他想是法语——反正不是英语。据我领会，那双眼睛和那张脸万分慑人，那声音却要说的话，倒是友善的。它告诉他，他危在顷刻，唯一的生机在于全然的不动声色。他若试图行使自己的意志，便死定了；至于那“死定”意味着什么，那声音给出了足够明白的示意，把他那身倦怠激成了阵阵孩子气的惊恐。“你的身体虚弱得动弹不得，”那声音说，“因为安拉已把手按在了它上面。”果然，特平发觉自己连一只小猫的气力都没有。“你已把你的意志交给了安拉，直到他归还给你。”这也属实，因为特平知道，除非有人命令，他连梳头的气力也召集不起来。“你会平安无事，”那声音说，“只要你睡着。你会一直睡到我叫你醒来。”

===Original===
Sleep he probably did, for once again came a big gap in his consciousness.﻿ ﻿… The next he knew he was being jolted in something that ran on wheels, and he suddenly rolled over on his side, as the vehicle took a sharp turn. This time it didn’t take him quite so long to wake up. He found he was in a big motorcar, with his overcoat on, and his hat on the seat beside him. He was stretched out almost at full length, and comfortably propped up with cushions. All this he realised fairly soon, but it was some time before he could gather up the past, and then it was all blurred and sketchy.﻿ ﻿… What he remembered most clearly was the warning that he was in grave peril and was only safe while he did nothing. That was burned in on his mind, and the lesson was pointed by the complete powerlessness of his limbs. He could hardly turn over from his side to his back, and he knew that if he attempted to stand he would fall down in a heap. He shut his eyes and tried to think.

===Chinese===
他多半真睡着了，因为他的意识又断了一大截。……再有知觉时，他正被什么带轮子的东西颠着，车身猛一拐弯，他忽地翻倒向一侧。这一回他醒来没用多久。他发觉自己在一辆大汽车里，大衣穿在身上，帽子搁在身旁的座位上。他几乎伸直了腿躺着，靠垫把他舒舒服服地架住。这些他很快就弄清了，可把过去的事收拢起来，又费了好一阵子，而且都模糊零碎。……他记得最清楚的，是那句警告：他身陷大险，唯有无所作为才得安全。这已烙在他心上，而四肢彻底的无力，又给这一课添了注脚。他从侧身翻到仰卧都吃力，他知道，若想站起来，就会瘫倒成一堆。他闭上眼睛，努力思索。

===Original===
Bit by bit the past pieced itself together. He remembered Archie’s cry﻿—and things before that﻿—Mary﻿—the girl in green. Very soon the truth smote him in the face. He had been kidnapped like the rest, and had had the same tricks played on him.﻿ ﻿… But they had only affected his body. As he realised this tremendous fact Turpin swelled with pride. Some devilry had stolen his physical strength, but his soul was his own still, his memory and his will. A sort of miasma of past fear still clung about him, like the aftertaste of influenza, but this only served to make him angry. He was most certainly not going to be beaten. The swine had miscalculated this time; they might have a cripple in their hands, but it would be a very watchful, wary, and determined cripple, quick to seize the first chance to be even with them. His anger made his spirits rise. All his life he had been a man of tropical loves and tempestuous hates. He had loathed the Boche, and free-masons, and communists and the deputies of his own land, and ever since Adela’s disappearance he had nursed a fury against a person or persons unknown: and now every detestation of which he was capable had been focused against those who were responsible for this night’s work. The fools! They thought they had got a trussed sheep, when all the time it was a lame tiger.

===Chinese===
往事一点点自己拼拢来。他记起阿奇的那声喊——还有更早的事——玛丽——那个绿衣姑娘。很快，真相迎面掴了他一记。他同其余人一样遭了绑架，一样的伎俩也使到了他身上。……可那些只伤及他的身体。当特平意识到这一重大事实时，他胸中豪气顿生。某种邪术偷走了他的体力，可他的灵魂仍是他自己的，他的记忆和他的意志。一层旧日恐惧的瘴气还黏在他身上，像流感的余味，但这只叫他火起。他断然不打算认输。这伙猪猡这回失算了；他们手里或许攥着一个残废，但那将是一个极警醒、极戒备、极有主意的残废，一逮着机会就要同他们算账。怒火反而振作了他的精神。他这辈子，向来是个爱起来如热带、恨起来如风暴的人。他憎恶过德国佬，憎恶过共济会员、共产党人，还有他自己国家的那些议员；自阿德拉失踪以来，他又对某个或某些不知名的人怀着满腔怒火：而此刻，他所能施出的每一分憎恶，都聚了焦，对准了这一夜勾当的元凶。这群蠢货！他们自以为捆住了一只羊，从头到尾那却是一头瘸了腿的老虎。

===Original===
The blinds of the car were down, but by small painful movements he managed to make out that there was a man in the front seat beside the chauffeur. By and by he got a corner of the right-hand blind raised, and saw that it was night time and that they were moving through broad streets that looked like a suburb. From the beat of the engine he gathered that the car was a Rolls-Royce, but not, he thought, one of the latest models. Presently the motion became less regular, and he realised that the suburban streets were giving place to country roads. His many expeditions in his Delage had taught him a good deal about the ways out of London, but, try as he might, he could not pick up any familiar landmark. The young moon had set, so he assumed that it was near midnight; it was a fine, clear night, not very dark, and he picked up an occasional inn and church, but they never seemed to pass through any village. Probably the driver was taking the less frequented roads﻿—a view he was confirmed in by the frequent right-angled turns and the many patches of indifferent surface.

===Chinese===
车的遮帘都放下了，但凭着一次次微小的、费劲的动作，他还是弄清了司机旁边的前座上坐着一个人。过了一阵，他把右手边遮帘的一角掀了起来，看出是夜里，车正穿过宽阔的、像是郊区的街道。从发动机的声响他听出这是辆劳斯莱斯，不过他想，不是最新款。不久车行得不那么匀顺了，他明白郊区街道已经换成了乡间道路。他乘自己的德拉热（Delage）出游过许多回，对出伦敦的各条路径颇知道些，可任凭怎么努力，他也认不出一个熟悉的地标。新月已经落了，他推想时近午夜；夜晴而清，不算很黑，偶尔望见一家客栈、一座教堂，可车仿佛从不穿过任何村庄。多半是司机专拣冷僻的路走——频繁的直角转弯和许多段不平整的路面，让他更加确信这一点。

===Original===
Very soon he found his efforts at reconnaissance so painful that he gave them up, and contented himself with planning his policy. Of course he must play the part of the witless sheep. That duty, he thought, presented no difficulties, for he rather fancied himself as an actor. The trouble was his bodily condition. He did not believe that a constitution as good as his could have taken any permanent damage from the night’s work.﻿ ﻿… The night’s! He must have been away for more than one night, for the row with Archie had taken place very near twelve o’clock. This must be the midnight following. He wondered what Mr. Victor was thinking about it﻿—and Mary﻿—and Hannay. The miserable Hannay had now four lost ones to look for instead of three!﻿ ﻿… Anyhow the devils had got an ugly prisoner in him. His body must soon be all right, unless of course they took steps to keep it all wrong. At that thought Turpin’s jaw set. The role of the docile sheep might be difficult to keep up very long.

===Chinese===
他很快发觉，侦察的努力太吃苦头，索性放弃了，一心盘算自己的方略。他自然得扮演那头没脑子的羊。这项任务，他想，毫无难处，因为他对自己当演员的本事还颇有几分自许。麻烦的是他的身体状况。他不信自己这样一副好体格，会被这一夜的勾当留下什么永久的损伤。——这一夜！他离场必定不止一夜了，因为同阿奇吵翻那阵子，时间已极近十二点。此刻必是紧接着的那个午夜。他不知道维克多先生对此作何想法——还有玛丽——还有汉内。倒霉的汉内，如今要找的失踪者从三个变成了四个！……反正，这帮魔鬼抓到手的，是个难缠的俘虏。他的身体很快就会复原，除非他们另行设法叫它一直坏下去。想到这里，特平咬紧了牙关。这头驯顺的羊，怕是不容易一直扮下去。

===Original===
The next he knew the car had turned in at a gate and was following a dark tree-lined avenue. In another minute it had stopped before the door of a house, and he was being lifted out by the chauffeur and the man from the front seat, and carried into a hall. But first a dark bandana was tied over his eyes, and, as he could do nothing with his arms or legs, he had to submit. He felt himself carried up a short staircase, and then along a corridor into a bedroom, where a lamp was lit. Hands undressed him﻿—his eyes still bandaged﻿—and equipped him with pyjamas which were not his own, and were at once too roomy and too short. Then food was brought, and an English voice observed that he had better have some supper before going to sleep. The bandage was taken off and he saw two male backs disappearing through the door.

===Chinese===
再往下一件事，是车拐进了一道大门，沿一条黑黢黢的林荫车道行驶。又过了一分钟，车停在一栋房子门前，司机和前座那人把他抬下车，抬进一个门厅。但先有一条黑头巾蒙上了他的眼睛，他手脚都使不上劲，只得听凭摆布。他觉出自己被抬上一段短楼梯，又沿一条走廊进了一间卧室，那里点着一盏灯。几只手替他脱了衣裳——眼睛还蒙着——给他套上一身不是他自己的睡衣裤，又肥又短。随后送来了吃食，一个英国口音说，他睡前最好用点晚饭。蒙眼布解了下来，他看见两条男人的背影正消失在门外。

===Original===
Up till now he had felt no hunger or thirst, but the sight of food made him realise that he was as empty as a drum. By twisting his head he could see it all laid out on the table beside his bed﻿—a good meal it looked﻿—cold ham and galantine, an omelette, a salad, cheese and a small decanter of red wine. His soul longed for it, but what about his feeble limbs? Was this some new torture of Tantalus?

===Chinese===
直到此刻他一直不觉得饥渴，可一看见食物，才发觉自己空得像面鼓。他扭过头去，就能看见吃食全摆在床边桌上——瞧着是一顿好饭——冷火腿和肉冻（galantine），一份煎蛋卷，一份沙拉，干酪，还有一小细颈瓶红酒。他的灵魂渴望着它，可他那副虚弱的肢体呢？这莫非是坦塔罗斯（Tantalus）的新式酷刑？

===Original===
Desire grew, and like an automaton he moved to it. He felt all numbed, with needles and pins everywhere, but surely he was less feeble than he had been in the car. First he managed to get his right arm extended, and by flexing the elbow and wrist a certain life seemed to creep back. Then he did the same thing with his right leg, and presently found that he could wriggle by inches to the edge of the bed. He was soon out of breath, but there could be no doubt about it﻿—he was getting stronger. A sudden access of thirst enabled him to grasp the decanter, and, after some trouble with the stopper, to draw it to his lips. Spilling a good deal, he succeeded in getting a mouthful. “Larose,” he murmured, “and a good vintage. It would have been better if it had been cognac.”

===Chinese===
欲望涨起来，他像具自动人偶似的朝它挪动。他浑身发麻，处处如针扎，但他确实比在车里的时候有劲了。他先把右臂伸直了，屈伸了几下肘和腕，一点生气仿佛悄悄爬了回来。然后他又如法炮制右腿，不多时便发现自己能一寸一寸蹭到床边。他很快就气喘吁吁，但这错不了——他正在恢复气力。一阵突来的干渴帮他攥住了那细颈瓶，同瓶塞搏斗了一番之后，把它举到了唇边。洒掉了不少，他总算喝到了一口。“拉罗斯（Larose），”他咕哝道，“还是个好年份。若是干邑就更好了。”

===Original===
But the wine put new life into him. He found he could use both arms, and he began wolfishly on the omelette, making a rather messy job of it. By this time he was feeling a remarkably vigorous convalescent, and he continued with the cold meat, till the cramp in his left shoulder forced him to lie back on the pillow. It soon passed, and he was able in fair comfort to finish the meal down to the last lettuce leaf of the salad, and the last drop of the claret. The Turpin who reclined again on the bed was to all intents the same vigorous young man who the night before had stumbled through that fateful door into the darkness. But it was a Turpin with a profoundly mystified mind.

===Chinese===
可这酒给他注入了新的生命。他发觉两条胳膊都能使了，便朝那份煎蛋卷狼吞虎咽地开了工，吃得相当狼藉。到这时，他自觉已是个精力旺盛得惊人的康复期病人，接着又对付冷肉，直到左肩的抽筋逼他躺回枕上。抽筋很快过去，他得以相当舒服地把这顿饭吃到最后一叶沙拉里的生菜、最后一滴红酒。重新靠回床上的那个特平，实质上已经又是前夜跌撞着穿过那扇命定之门、陷入黑暗的那个精力充沛的年轻人了。只是这个特平，心里装着一大团解不开的疑团。

===Original===
He would have liked to smoke, but his cigarettes were in the pocket of his dress clothes which had been removed. So he started to do for his legs what he had already achieved for his arms, and with the same happy results. It occurred to him that, while he was alone, he had better discover whether or not he could stand. He made the effort, rolled out of bed on to the floor, hit the little table with his head and set the dishes rattling.

===Chinese===
他本想抽支烟，可香烟在被拿走的晚礼服口袋里。于是他开始给两条腿做先前给两条胳膊做过的那套功课，成效同样可喜。他忽然想到，趁屋里没人，最好弄清自己到底还能不能站。他一使劲，从床上滚落到地板上，脑袋撞上小桌，碟盘叮当乱响。

===Original===
But after a few scrambles he got to his feet and managed to shuffle round the room. The mischief was leaving his body﻿—so much was plain, and but for a natural stiffness in the joints he felt as well as ever. But what it all meant he hadn’t a notion. He was inclined to the belief that somehow he had scored off his enemies, and was a tougher proposition than they had bargained for. They had assuredly done no harm to his mind with their witchcraft, and it looked as if they had also failed with his body. The thought emboldened him. The house seemed quiet; why should he not do a little exploration?

===Chinese===
但连滚带爬折腾了几下，他还是站了起来，蹭着步子在房里挪了一圈。那邪祟正在离开他的身体——这一点很明显，除了关节天然的发僵，他自觉同从前一样好。可这一切究竟意味着什么，他毫无头绪。他倾向于相信，自己总算在敌人身上占了一分便宜，是块比他们预算中难啃的硬骨头。他们的妖术确实没伤着他的头脑，看样子在他的身体上也失了手。这念头给他壮了胆。屋子里似乎很静；他何不稍事侦察一番？

===Original===
He cautiously opened the door, finding it, somewhat to his surprise, unlocked. The passage was lit by a hanging oil-lamp, carpeted with an old-fashioned drugget, and its walls decorated with a set of flower pictures. Turpin came to the conclusion that rarely in his life had he been in a dwelling which seemed more innocent and homelike. He considered himself sensitive to the *nuances* of the sinister in an atmosphere, and there was nothing of that sort in this. He took a step or two down the passage, and then halted, for he thought he heard a sound. Yes, there could be no doubt of it. It was water gushing from a tap. Someone in the establishment was about to have a bath.

===Chinese===
他小心翼翼地开了门，颇有些意外地发现门没锁。走廊里点着一盏悬吊的油灯，铺着老式粗呢地毯，墙上挂着一组花卉图画。特平得出结论：他一生中很少进过比这更像良善人家的宅子。他自认为对一种气氛里阴森的*微妙气息（nuances）*感觉灵敏，而这宅子里半点那类东西也没有。他沿走廊走了一两步便停住了，因为他觉得听见了声响。是的，错不了。是水从龙头里哗哗涌出。这宅子里的什么人正打算洗澡。

===Original===
Then he slipped back to his room just in time. The someone was approaching with light feet and a rustle of draperies. He had his door shut when the steps passed, and then opened it and stuck his head out. He saw a pink dressing-gown, and above it a slender neck and masses of dark hair. It was the figure which he of all men was likely to know best.

===Chinese===
他及时缩回自己房里。那个“什么人”正轻着脚步走来，衣料窸窣作响。脚步经过时他已关上门，随后又开门探出头去。他看见一件粉色晨衣，晨衣之上是一段纤细的脖颈和一头浓密的黑发。这副身形，普天下男人里，要数他最不可能认错。

===Original===
It seemed that the place for him was bed, so he got between the sheets again and tried to think. Adela Victor was here; therefore he was in the hands of her captors, and made a fourth in their bag. But what insanity had prompted these wary criminals to bring the two of them to the same prison? Were they so utterly secure, so confident of their power, that they took this crazy risk? The insolence of it made him furious. In the name of every saint he swore that he would make them regret it. He would free the lady and himself, though he had to burn down the house and wring the neck of every inmate. And then he remembered the delicacy of the business, and the need of exact timing if the other two hostages were not to be lost, and at the thought he groaned.

===Chinese===
看来他该待的地方是床上，于是他重新钻进被窝，试着思索。阿德拉·维克多在这里；那么他就在掳走她的那伙人手里，成了他们猎袋里的第四件猎物。可是什么失心疯，驱使这伙谨慎的罪犯把他们俩弄进同一座监狱？他们当真有恃无恐到这地步、对自己的力量自信到这地步，竟敢冒这种疯狂的险？这份狂妄把他气得火冒三丈。他凭着历代圣徒之名起誓，定要叫他们后悔。他要救出小姐和他自己，哪怕烧掉这栋房子、拧断里面每一个人的脖子。可随即他想起了这桩事体的干系微妙，想起若要保住另外两名人质，时机分毫差不得——想到这里，他呻吟了一声。

===Original===
There was a tap at the door, and a man entered to clear away the supper table. He seemed an ordinary English valet, with his stiff collar and decent black coat and smug expressionless face.

===Chinese===
门上轻敲了一下，一个男人进来收拾晚饭桌。他看着是个寻常的英国贴身男仆，硬领，体面的黑外套，一张自矜而呆板的脸。

===Original===
“Beg pardon, my lord,” he said, “at what hour would you like your shavin’ water? Seein’ it’s been a late night I make so bold as to suggest ten o’clock.”

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，爵爷，”他说，“您几点钟要刮脸水？想着昨儿夜里晚了，小的斗胆向您提议十点钟。”

===Original===
Turpin assented, and the servant had hardly gone when another visitor appeared. It was a slim pale man, whom he was not conscious of having seen before, a man with grey hair and a melancholy droop of the head. He stood at the foot of the bed, gazing upon the prostrate Turpin, and his look was friendly. Then he addressed him in French of the most Saxon type.

===Chinese===
特平应了，仆人前脚刚走，另一位来客就到了。是个瘦削苍白的男人，他不记得先前见过——灰白头发，头颅忧郁地垂着。他站在床脚，俯视着躺着的特平，神色是友善的。随后他开口对他说话，说的是一口撒克逊味十足的法语。

===Original===
“*Êtes-vous comfortable, monsieur? C’est bien. Soyez tranquille. Nous sommes vos amis. Bon soir.*”

===Chinese===
“您还舒适吗，先生？很好。请安心。我们是您的朋友。晚安。”

## XI How a German Engineer Found Strange Fishing / 十一、一个德国工程师如何发现了奇特的钓场
===Original===
I got to Hull about six o’clock, having left my car at a garage in York, and finished the journey by train. I had my kit in a small suitcase and rucksack, and I waited on the quay till I saw Dr. Newhover arrive with a lot of luggage and a big rod-box. When I reckoned he would be in his cabin arranging his belongings, I went on board myself, and went straight to my own cabin, which was a comfortable two-berthed one well forward. There I had sandwiches brought me, and settled myself to doze and read for thirty-six hours.

===Chinese===
我把汽车留在约克（York）一家车库，改乘火车走完剩下的旅程，六点来钟到了赫尔（Hull）。我的行装是一口小提箱和一只背包。我在码头上候着，直到望见纽霍弗医生带着一大堆行李和一只大钓竿箱到来。我估摸他这会儿该在舱房里归置物件了，才自己登船，径直进了我的舱房——那是间舒服的双铺舱房，位置很靠船头。我在那里叫人送来三明治，安顿下来，预备打盹读书，消磨三十六个钟头。

===Original===
All that night and all next day it blew fairly hard, and I remained quietly in my bunk, trying to read Boswell’s *Life of Johnson*, and thanking my stars that I hadn’t lived a thousand years earlier and been a Viking. I didn’t see myself ploughing those short steep seas in an open galley. I woke on the morning of the 23rd to find the uneasy motion at an end, and, looking out of my porthole, saw a space of green sunlit water, a rocky beach, and the white and red of a little town. The *Gudrun* waited about an hour at Stavanger, so I gave Dr. Newhover time to get on shore, before I had a hurried breakfast in the saloon and followed him. I saw him go off with two men, and get on board a motor-launch which was lying beside one of the jetties. The coast was now clear, so I went into the town, found the agents to whom Archie Roylance had cabled, and learned that my own motor-launch was ready and waiting in the inner harbour where the fishing-boats lie. A clerk took me down there, and introduced me to Johan, my skipper, a big, cheerful, bearded Norwegian, who had a smattering of English. I bought a quantity of provisions, and by ten o’clock we were on the move. I asked Johan about the route to Merdal, and he pointed out a moving speck a couple of miles ahead of us. “That is Kristian Egge’s boat,” he said. “He carries an English fisherman to Merdal and we follow.” I got my glasses on the craft, and made out Newhover smoking in the stern.

===Chinese===
一整夜加上第二天一整天，风都刮得相当紧。我安安静静待在铺位上，试着读鲍斯韦尔（Boswell）的《约翰逊传》（*Life of Johnson*），一面暗自庆幸，幸亏没生在一千年前当维京人——我可看不出自己有什么本事，能驾着一艘敞篷长船去犁开那样又短又陡的浪头。23日清晨我醒来，发觉那阵叫人不安的颠簸已经停了；从舷窗望出去，是一大片洒满阳光的绿水、一道岩石海滩，还有一座小镇里白红相间的房舍。“古德伦”号（*Gudrun*）要在斯塔万格（Stavanger）停约一个钟头，我便由着纽霍弗医生先上岸，自己在餐舱里匆匆用过早饭，再跟下去。我望见他同两个男人一起离开，登上一条泊在突堤旁的汽艇。这下再无人碍眼，我进了城，寻到阿奇·罗伊伦斯打过电报的那家代理行，得知我自己雇的汽艇已经备好，正泊在渔船停靠的内港里等着。一名职员领我下去，把我引荐给我的艇长约翰（Johan）——一个高大快活、蓄着胡子的挪威人，能说几句皮毛英语。我采买了大批食物，到十点钟我们就动身了。我问约翰去梅达尔（Merdal）的航路，他朝我们前方两英里外一个移动的小黑点指了指。“那是克里斯蒂安·埃格（Kristian Egge）的船，”他说，“他载一个英国钓鱼的上梅达尔去，我们就跟着。”我举起望远镜对准那条船，认出了坐在船尾抽烟的纽霍弗。

===Original===
It was a gorgeous day, with that funny Northern light which makes noon seem like early morning. I enjoyed every hour of it, partly because I had now a definite job before me, and partly because I was in the open air to which I properly belonged. I got no end of amusement watching the wild life﻿—the cormorants and eider-duck on the little islands, and the seals, with heads as round as Medina’s, that slipped off the skerries at our approach. The air was chilly and fresh, but when we turned the corner of the Merdalfjord out of the sea-wind and the sun climbed the sky it was as warm as June. A big flat island we passed, all short turf and rocky outcrops, was pointed out to me by Johan as Flacksholm. Soon we were shaping due east in an inlet which was surrounded by dark steep hills, with the snow lying in the gullies. I had Boswell with me in two volumes; the first I had read in the steamer, and the second I was now starting on, when it fell overboard, through my getting up in a hurry to look at a flock of duck. So I presented the odd volume to Johan, and surrendered myself to tobacco and meditation.

===Chinese===
那天天气好极了，带着北方独有的古怪光线，正午也像清早。一路每个钟头我都受用，一半因为眼下有了一件明确的差事，一半因为我又回到了露天里——那本才是我安身的地方。观赏野生动物给了我无穷的乐趣：小岛上栖着鸬鹚和绒鸭，还有海豹——脑袋圆得同梅迪纳的一样——见我们驶近，便从礁岛上滑进水里。空气清冷而新鲜，可等我们绕过梅达尔峡湾（Merdalfjord）的拐角、避开海风，太阳升上高空，暖得竟像六月。我们经过一座平坦的大岛，满岛短草和裸露的岩石，约翰指给我说，那是弗拉克斯霍尔姆（Flacksholm）。不多时我们折向正东，驶入一道水湾，两岸尽是幽暗陡峭的山，沟壑里积着雪。我随身带着两卷本的鲍斯韦尔；第一卷在轮船上读完了，第二卷正要开卷，却因为我慌忙起身去看一群鸭子，掉进了海里。于是我把这一册孤本送给约翰，自己则交给烟草和玄想去打理。

===Original===
In the afternoon the inlet narrowed to a fjord, and the walls of hill grew steeper. They were noble mountains, cut sharp like the edge of the Drakensberg, and crowned with a line of snow, so that they looked like a sugarcoated cake that had been sliced. Streams came out of the upper snow-wreaths and hurled themselves down the steeps﻿—above a shimmering veil of mist, and below a torrent of green water tumbling over pebbles to the sea. The landscape and the weather lulled me into a delectable peace which refused to be disturbed by any “looking before or after,” as some poet says. Newhover was ahead of me﻿—we never lost track of his launch﻿—and it was my business to see what he was up to and to keep myself out of his sight. The ways and means of it I left to fortune to provide.

===Chinese===
午后，水湾收窄成一道峡湾，山壁愈发陡峭。那都是些气派十足的山，棱角切得像德拉肯斯堡（Drakensberg）的山缘一样利落，顶上覆着一线雪，看上去就像一只切开了的糖霜蛋糕。溪流从高处的雪环里钻出，朝陡坡直扑下来——上半截是一幅微微闪烁的雾纱，下半截是一股碧绿的急流，翻滚着越过卵石奔向大海。这山水和这天气把我哄入一种甜美的安宁，任凭什么“前瞻与后顾”——某位诗人这么说的——都搅扰不得。纽霍弗在我前头——我们始终没有跟丢他的汽艇——而我的差事，就是看看他在干什么名堂，同时别叫他瞧见我。至于用什么法子，我交给时运去筹办。

===Original===
By and by the light grew dimmer, and the fjord grew narrower, so that dusk fell on us, though, looking back down the inlet, we could see a bright twilight. I assumed that Newhover would go on to Merdal and the fjord’s head, where the Skarso entered the sea, and had decided to stop at Hauge, a village two miles short of it, on the south shore. We came to Hauge about half-past eight, in a wonderful purple dusk, for the place lay right under the shadow of a great cliff. I gave Johan full instructions: he was to wait for me and expect me when I turned up, and to provision himself from the village. On no account must he come up to Merdal, or go out of sight or hail of the boat. He seemed to relish the prospect of a few days’ idleness, for he landed me at a wooden jetty in great good-humour, and wished me sport. What he thought I was after I cannot imagine, for I departed with a rucksack on my back and a stout stick in my hand, which scarcely suggested the chase.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，光线暗下来，峡湾也窄起来，夜色落到我们头上；只是回头望向水湾下游，还看得见一片明亮的曙暮光。我料定纽霍弗会一直去梅达尔，去峡湾顶端斯卡索河（the Skarso）入海的地方，便决定停在豪格（Hauge）——一个离峡湾头还差两英里的村子，在南岸。约莫八点半，我们到了豪格，正逢一种妙不可言的紫色暮霭，因为这地方就躺在一座大悬崖的阴影底下。我给约翰交代周详：他就在此地等我，我几时露面他几时接应，吃食从村里自备；万万不可上梅达尔去，也不可走开到看不见船、喊不应人的地步。他似乎很乐意享几天清闲，兴高采烈地在一座木头突堤边让我上岸，还祝我行猎尽兴。他心里当我出来做什么，我实在想不出，因为我背着一只背包、手里拄一根粗手杖上路，怎么也不像行猎的打扮。

===Original===
I was in good spirits myself as I stretched my legs on the road which led from Hauge to Merdal. The upper fjord lay black on my left hand, the mountains rose black on my right, but though I walked in darkness I could see twilight ahead of me, where the hills fell back from the Skarso valley, that wonderful apple-green twilight which even in spring is all the northern night. I had never seen it before, and I suppose something in my blood answered to the place﻿—for my father used to say that the Hannays came originally from Norse stock. There was a jolly crying of birds from the waters, ducks and geese and oystercatchers and sandpipers, and now and then would come a great splash as if a salmon were jumping in the brackish tides on his way to the Skarso. I was thinking longingly of my rods left behind, when on turning a corner the lights of Merdal showed ahead, and it seemed to me that I had better be thinking of my next step.

===Chinese===
我也兴致颇高，在从豪格通往梅达尔的路上舒展腿脚。上游的峡湾在左手边一片黑，群山在右手边黑压压地立着；但我虽走在黑暗里，前头却望得见亮光，就在群山朝斯卡索河谷退开的地方——那种妙极了的苹果绿暮色，即便在春天，也便是北方之夜的全部了。这光景我从前没有见过，我想是我血液里有什么东西在同这地方应和——先父在世时常说，汉内家最早原是北欧人的种。水面上响着一片欢快的鸟声，有鸭、有雁、有蛎鹬、有矶鹬，不时还传来哗啦一声大响，仿佛一尾鲑鱼正逆着微咸的潮水跃起，赶路去斯卡索河。我正怅然惦念留在家里没带的钓竿，一拐弯，梅达尔的灯火已在前头露面，我这才觉得，还是先想想下一步的好。

===Original===
I knew no Norwegian, but I counted on finding natives who could speak English, seeing so many of them have been in England or America. Newhover, I assumed, would go to the one hotel, and it was for me to find lodgings elsewhere. I began to think this spying business might be more difficult than I had thought, for if he saw me he would recognise me, and that must not happen. I was ready, of course, with a story of a walking tour, but he would be certain to suspect, and certain to let Medina know.﻿ ﻿… Well, a lodging for the night was my first business, and I must start inquiries. Presently I came to the little pier of Merdal, which was short of the village itself. There were several men sitting smoking on barrels and coils of rope, and one who stood at the end looking out to where Kristian Egge’s boat, which had brought Newhover, lay moored. I turned down the road to it, for it seemed a place to gather information.

===Chinese===
挪威话我一个字不会，但指望找到会讲英语的本地人——毕竟他们去英国、去美国的那么多。我估摸纽霍弗会住那家唯一的旅馆，我便得在别处觅宿。我开始觉得，这桩盯梢的差事兴许比我想的难办：他只要见我一眼就能认出来，而这万万不可。当然，我有一套徒步旅行的说辞现成备用，可他必定起疑，也必定报给梅迪纳。……好吧，头一件事是寻个过夜的宿处，得赶紧打听。不多时我来到梅达尔的小码头，它就在村子跟前、还没到村子处。有几条汉子坐在木桶和绳圈上抽烟，还有一个站在码头尽头，朝外望向克里斯蒂安·埃格那条船——就是载纽霍弗来的那条——系泊的地方。我折下通往码头的路，因为那似乎是个收罗消息的去处。

===Original===
I said good evening to the men, and was just about to ask them for advice about quarters, when the man who had been looking out to sea turned round at the sound of my voice. He seemed an oldish fellow, with rather a stoop in his back, wearing an ancient shooting-jacket. The light was bad, but there was something in the cut of his jib that struck me as familiar, though I couldn’t put a name to it.

===Chinese===
我向那几条汉子道了晚上好，正要向他们讨教宿处的主意，那个一直望着海的人听见我的声音，转过身来。他像是个上了年纪的人，背相当驼，穿一件老旧的猎装。光线不好，但他那副眉眼做派里有点什么叫我眼熟，一时却又叫不出名目来。

===Original===
I spoke to the Norwegians in English, but it was obvious that I had hit on a bunch of indifferent linguists. They shook their heads, and one pointed to the village, as if to tell me that I would be better understood there. Then the man in the shooting-jacket spoke.

===Chinese===
我用英语同那些挪威人搭话，可显然我碰上的是一伙不高明的语言学家。他们直摇头，有一个朝村子里指了指，像是在告诉我，到那边人家更听得懂。这时那个穿猎装的人开了口。

===Original===
“Perhaps I can help,” he said. “There is a good inn in Merdal, which at this season is not full.”

===Chinese===
“或许我能帮上忙，”他说。“梅达尔有家好客栈，这个季节还没住满。”

===Original===
He spoke excellent English, but it was obvious that he wasn’t an Englishman. There was an unmistakable emphasis of the gutturals.

===Chinese===
他英语讲得极好，但一耳朵就听得出不是英国人。喉音里有一种错不了的加重。

===Original===
“I doubt the inn may be too good for my purse,” I said. “I am on a walking-tour and must lodge cheaply.”

===Chinese===
“只怕那客栈好得超出了我的钱袋，”我说。“我是徒步旅行的，必须住得省。”

===Original===
He laughed pleasantly. “There may be accommodation elsewhere. Peter Bojer may have a spare bed. I am going that way, sir, and can direct you.”

===Chinese===
他愉快地笑了。“别处兴许也有下处。彼得·博耶尔（Peter Bojer）家可能有张空床。我正往那边去，先生，可以给你指路。”

===Original===
He had turned towards me, and his figure caught the beam of the riding-light of the motor-launch. I saw a thin sunburnt face with a very pleasant expression, and an untidy grizzled beard. Then I knew him, and I could have shouted with amazement at the chance which had brought us two together again.

===Chinese===
他已转过身来朝着我，身影正吃上汽艇桅灯的光柱。我看见一张瘦削的、晒黑了的脸，神情十分和善，还有一部蓬乱的斑白胡子。这下我认出他了；机缘竟又把我们俩凑到一处，我惊得险些叫出声来。

===Original===
We walked side by side up the jetty road and on to the highway.

===Chinese===
我们并肩走上突堤的路，上了大路。

===Original===
“I think,” I said, “that we have met before, Herr Gaudian.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”我说，“我们从前见过面吧，高迪安先生（Herr Gaudian）。”

===Original===
He stopped short. “That is my name﻿ ﻿… but I do not﻿ ﻿… I do not think﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
他猛地站住。“那是我的名字……可我不……我不认为……”

===Original===
“Do you remember a certain Dutchman called Cornelius Brandt whom you entertained at your country house one night in December ’15?”

===Chinese===
“你可记得一个叫科尼利厄斯·勃兰特（Cornelius Brandt）的荷兰人？1915年12月的一个晚上，你在乡间宅邸款待过他。”

===Original===
He looked searchingly in my face.

===Chinese===
他探究地端详我的脸。

===Original===
“I remember,” he said. “I also remember a Mr. Richard Hanau, one of Guggenheim’s engineers, with whom I talked at Constantinople.”

===Chinese===
“我记得，”他说。“我还记得一位理查德·哈瑙先生（Mr. Richard Hanau），古根海姆（Guggenheim）手下的工程师，我在君士坦丁堡（Constantinople）同他谈过话。”

===Original===
“The same,” I said. For a moment I was not clear how he was going to take the revelation, but his next action reassured me, and I saw that I had not been wrong in my estimate of the one German I have ever wholeheartedly liked. He began to laugh, a friendly tolerant laugh.

===Chinese===
“正是同一人，”我说。一时之间我摸不准他对这层揭破会作何感想，但他接下来的举动让我放了心，也证明我对这个德国人的品评没有看走眼——他是我有生以来唯一打心眼里喜欢的德国人。他放声笑了，一种友好的、宽容的笑。

===Original===
“*Kritzi Turken!*” he cried. “It is indeed romantic. I have often wondered whether I should see or hear of you again, and behold! you step out of the darkness on a Norwegian fjord.”

===Chinese===
“*Kritzi Turken*！”他叫道。“这可真够浪漫的。我常想，此生还能不能再见你一面、再听到你的消息，你瞧！你竟从黑暗里走出来，站到了挪威的峡湾上。”

===Original===
“You bear no malice?” I said. “I served my country as you served yours. I played fair, as you played fair.”

===Chinese===
“你不记仇吧？”我说。“我为我的国家效力，正如你为你的国家效力。我行事磊落，正如你行事磊落。”

===Original===
“Malice!” he cried. “But we are gentlemen; also we are not children. I rejoice to see that you have survived the War. I have always wished you well, for you are a very bold and brave man.”

===Chinese===
“记仇！”他叫道。“咱们是君子，再说咱们也不是小孩子。见你从大战里活了下来，我很欢喜。我一向愿你安好，因为你是个非常大胆勇敢的人。”

===Original===
“Not a bit of it,” I said﻿—“only lucky.”

===Chinese===
“哪里的话，”我说——“不过是运气好罢了。”

===Original===
“By what name shall I call you now﻿—Brandt or Hanau?”

===Chinese===
“如今我该怎样称呼你——勃兰特，还是哈瑙？”

===Original===
“My name is Richard Hannay, but for the present I am calling myself Cornelius Brand﻿—for a reason which I am going to tell you.” I had suddenly made up my mind to take Gaudian into my full confidence. He seemed to have been sent by Providence for that purpose, and I was not going to let such a chance slip.

===Chinese===
“我的名字是理查德·汉内，不过眼下我自称科尼利厄斯·勃兰德（Cornelius Brand）——缘由我正要讲给你听。”我突然打定主意，要向高迪安和盘托出。他简直像上天专程派来做这个的，这样的机会我不能白白放走。

===Original===
But at my words he stopped short.

===Chinese===
可听了我的话，他猛地站住。

===Original===
“Mr. Hannay,” he said, “I do not want your confidence. You are still engaged, I take it, in your country’s service? I do not question your motive, but remember I am a German, and I cannot be party to the pursuit of one of my countrymen, however base I may think him.”

===Chinese===
“汉内先生，”他说，“我不想听你的机密。我料想，你仍在为你的国家效力？我不怀疑你的动机，可是请记住，我是个德国人，追查我的一个同胞，这种事我不能沾手，不论我把那同胞看得多么卑劣。”

===Original===
I could only stare. “But I am not in my country’s service,” I stammered. “I left it at the Armistice, and I’m a farmer now.”

===Chinese===
我只能干瞪眼。“可我并没有为国家效力啊，”我结结巴巴地说。“停战那年我就退了役，如今是个庄稼人。”

===Original===
“Do English farmers travel in Norway under false names?”

===Chinese===
“英国的庄稼人也用假名字在挪威旅行？”

===Original===
“That’s a private business which I want to explain to you. I assure you there is no German in it. I want to keep an eye on the doings of a fashionable English doctor.”

===Chinese===
“那是一桩私事，我正想向你解释。我向你担保，这事情里头没有一个德国人。我要盯的是一个时髦英国医生的行径。”

===Original===
“I must believe you,” he said after a pause. “But two hours ago a man arrived in the launch you see anchored out there. He is a fisherman and is now at the inn. That man is known to me﻿—too well known. He is a German, who during the War served Germany in secret ways, in America and elsewhere. I did not love him and I think he did my country grievous ill, but that is a matter for us Germans to settle, and not for foreigners.”

===Chinese===
“我只好信你，”他顿了顿说。“可是两个钟头前，有个人搭你看见泊在外头的那条汽艇到了。他是个钓鱼的，眼下正在客栈里。那个人我认得——认得太清楚了。他是个德国人，大战期间在美国和别处用秘密路子替德国办过事。我不喜欢他，而且我认为他给我国带来了惨重的祸害，可那是我们德国人自家的事，轮不到外人来插手。”

===Original===
“I know your man as Dr. Newhover of Wimpole Street.”

===Chinese===
“你说的这个人，我知道他叫温波尔街的纽霍弗医生。”

===Original===
“So?” he said. “He has taken again his father’s name, which was Neuhofer. We knew him as Kristoffer. What do you want with him?”

===Chinese===
“哦？”他说。“他又用回了父亲的姓，本来是纽霍弗（Neuhofer）。我们从前认得他时，他叫克里斯托弗（Kristoffer）。你要他做什么？”

===Original===
“Nothing that any honest German wouldn’t approve,” and there and then I gave him a sketch of the Medina business. He exclaimed in horror.

===Chinese===
“我做的事，没有一件是正直的德国人不肯赞同的。”我当场把梅迪纳那桩事的始末给他讲了个大概。他骇然叫出声来。

===Original===
“Mr. Hannay,” he said hesitatingly, “you are being honest with me?”

===Chinese===
“汉内先生，”他迟疑地说，“你对我说的是实话？”

===Original===
“I swear by all that’s holy I am telling you the plain truth, and the full truth. Newhover may have done anything you jolly well like in the War. That’s all washed out. I’m after him to get a line on a foul business which is English in origin. I want to put a spoke in the wheel of English criminals, and to save innocent lives. Besides, Newhover is only a subordinate. I don’t propose to raise a hand against him, only to find out what he is doing.”

===Chinese===
“我指着一切神圣的事物起誓，我说的是老实话，是全部实情。纽霍弗在大战里干过什么，随你怎么说都行，那笔账早已勾销。我追他，是要摸一桩肮脏勾当的线索，那勾当源起英国。我要往英国罪犯的车轮里插一根辐条，还要救几条无辜的性命。再说，纽霍弗不过是个从属。我不打算动他一根指头，只想查明他在干什么。”

===Original===
He held out his hand. “I believe you,” he said, “and if I can I will help you.”

===Chinese===
他伸出手来。“我相信你，”他说，“只要办得到，我愿帮你。”

===Original===
He conducted me through the long street of the village, past the inn, where I supposed Newhover was now going to bed, and out on to the road which ran up the Skarso valley. We came in sight of the river, a mighty current full of melted snow, sweeping in noble curves through the meadowland in that uncanny dusk. It appeared that he lodged with Peter Bojer, who had a spare bed, and when we reached the cottage, which stood a hundred yards from the highway on the very brink of the stream, Peter was willing to let me have it. His wife gave us supper﻿—an omelette, smoked salmon, and some excellent Norwegian beer﻿—and after it I got out my map and had a survey of the neighbourhood.

===Chinese===
他领我穿过村里的长街，经过那家客栈——我想纽霍弗这时该上床了——上了沿斯卡索河谷而上的大路。我们望见了那条河：满满一河融雪的浩荡急流，在那幽异的暮色里，以壮阔的弧线扫过草场。原来他自己就寄宿在彼得·博耶尔家，家里有张空床；我们到那座村舍时——它离大路一百来码，就在河沿上——彼得愿意把空床让给我。他妻子给我们做了晚饭——一份煎蛋卷、熏鲑鱼，还有上好的挪威啤酒——饭后我摊开地图，把左近的地势察看了一番。

===Original===
Gaudian gave me a grisly picture of the condition of his own country. It seemed that the downfall of the old regime had carried with it the decent wise men like himself, who had opposed its follies, but had lined up with it on patriotic grounds when the War began. He said that Germany was no place for a moderate man, and that the power lay with the bloated industrials, who were piling up fortunes abroad while they were wrecking their country at home. The only opposition, he said, came from the communists, who were half-witted, and the monarchists, who wanted the impossible. “Reason is not listened to, and I fear there is no salvation till my poor people have passed through the last extremity. You foreign Powers have hastened our destruction, when you had it in your hands to save us. I think you have meant well, but you have been blind, for you have not supported our moderate men and have by your harshness played the game of the wreckers among us.”

===Chinese===
高迪安给我描绘了一幅他自己祖国境况的阴惨图景。旧政权垮台，似乎把像他这样正派而明智的人也一并带倒了——这些人当初反对过它的种种蠢行，可大战一开，又出于爱国心同它站到了一处。他说，德国如今不是温和派容身的地方，权柄握在那些脑满肠肥的实业家手里，他们在国外大笔攒钱，同时在国内把自己的国家拆得七零八落。他说，唯一的反对力量，一头是共产党人，半痴半呆；一头是保皇党人，想的是办不到的事。“理性没人肯听，我怕是要等我可怜的人民熬到山穷水尽的最后一着，才有得救的日子。你们外国列强本可以救我们，却加速了我们的毁灭。我想你们是好意，可你们瞎了眼：你们没有扶持我们的温和派，反倒用你们的苛刻，帮了我们中间拆家伙的人的忙。”

===Original===
It appeared that he was very poor now, like all the professional classes. I thought it odd that this man, who had a worldwide reputation as an engineer, couldn’t earn a big income in any country he chose. Then I saw that it was because he had lost the wish to make money. He had seen too deep into the vanity of human wishes to have any ambition left. He was unmarried, with no near relations, and he found his pleasure in living simply in remote country places and watching flowers and beasts. He was a keen fisherman, but couldn’t afford a good beat, so he leased a few hundred yards from a farmer, who had not enough water to get a proper rent for it, and he did a lot of trout fishing in the tarns high up in the hills and in the Skarso above the foss. As he sat facing me beyond the stove, with his kind sad brown eyes and his rugged face, I thought how like he was to a Scottish moorland shepherd. I had liked him when I first saw him in Stumm’s company, but now I liked him so much that because of him I was prepared to think better of the whole German race.

===Chinese===
看来他如今很穷，同所有自由职业阶层的人一样。我起初觉得奇怪：这个人身为工程师，名满天下，随便挑哪一个国家，还怕挣不来一份大收入？后来我明白了，那是因为他已失去挣钱的心思。他把人类欲望的虚妄看得太深，再剩不下什么雄心。他未婚，没有近亲，把乐趣放在僻远乡野间的简朴生活上，看看花草，看看走兽。他钓鱼成痴，却租不起好钓段，便向一个农夫租了几百码水面——那农夫的水不够租出一份像样的租金——又常到山高处的小湖里钓鳟，或是到斯卡索河瀑布以上的河段去下钩。他隔着炉子在我对面坐着，一双温厚的、含着忧色的棕色眼睛，一张粗粝的脸，我心里想，他多像一个苏格兰荒原上的牧羊人。当年我头一回见到他，是他在施图姆（Stumm）身边的时候，我就喜欢他；如今我喜欢他到了这个地步——因为他，我愿意把整个德意志民族都往好里多想一层。

===Original===
I asked him if he had heard of any other Englishman in the valley﻿—anyone of the name of Jason, for instance. He said no; he had been there for three weeks, but the fishing did not begin for another fortnight, and foreign visitors had not yet arrived. Then I asked him about the saeter farms, and he said that few of these were open yet, since the high pastures were not ready. One or two on the lower altitudes might be already inhabited, but not many, though the winter had been a mild one and the spring had come early. “Look at the Skarso,” he said. “Usually in April it is quite low, for the snowfields have not begun to melt. But today it is as brimming as if it were the middle of May.”

===Chinese===
我问他可曾听说这山谷里还有别的英国人——比如，有没有一个姓贾森（Jason）的。他说没有；他来此三个星期了，可钓期还要再过两个礼拜才开，外国客人尚未到。我又问起那些山间牧场，他说开张的还寥寥无几，因为高处的草场还没就绪。低海拔的一两处或许已有人住，但不多——尽管这个冬天暖和，春天又来得早。“看看斯卡索河，”他说。“往常到了四月，水位还低得很，因为雪原还没开始化。可今天这河水，满得像是到了五月中旬。”

===Original===
He went over the map with me﻿—an inch-to-a-mile one I had got in London﻿—and showed me the lie of the land. The saeters were mostly farther up the river, reached by paths up the tributary glens. There was a good road running the length of the valley, but no side roads to connect with the parallel glens, the Uradal and the Bremendal. I found indeed one track marked on the map, which led to the Uradal by a place called Snaasen. “Yes,” said Gaudian, “that is the only thing in the way of what you soldiers would call lateral communications. I’ve walked it, and I’m sorry for the man who tries the road in bad weather. You can see the beginning of the track from this house; it climbs up beside the torrent just across the valley. Snaasen is more or less inhabited all the year round, and I suppose you would call it a kind of saeter. It is a sort of shelter hut for travellers taking that road, and in summer it is a paradise for flowers. You would be surprised at the way the natives can cross the hills even in winter. Snaasen belongs to the big farm two miles upstream, which carries with it the best beat on the Skarso. Also there is said to be first-class ryper-shooting later in the year, and an occasional bear. By the way, I rather fancy someone told me that the whole thing was owned by, or had been leased to, an Englishman.﻿ ﻿… You are rich, you see, and you do not leave much in Norway for poor people.”

===Chinese===
他同我一起把地图过了一遍——那是我在伦敦买的一英寸比一英里的图——给我讲地势。山间牧场大多在河的上游更远处，要从支流峡谷的小径上去。谷地全长有一条好路，却没有岔道同平行的两条峡谷——乌拉达尔（Uradal）和布雷门达尔（Bremendal）——相通。我倒真在图上找到一条标出的小径，经由一个叫斯纳森（Snaasen）的地方通往乌拉达尔。“是啊，”高迪安说，“这便是你们军人所谓的横向交通线，仅此一条。我走过，谁要在坏天气里走那条道，我只有替他叫苦。从这屋子就望得见小径的起点；就在山谷正对面，贴着那道急流往上爬。斯纳森一年到头多少总有人住，我想你们会管它叫一种山间牧场。它是走那条路的旅人歇脚的棚屋，到了夏天便是一座花的天堂。本地人翻山越岭的本事，哪怕在冬天，也会叫你吃惊。斯纳森属于上游两英里的大农庄，连带着斯卡索河上最好的钓段。据说入秋之后，那里还有一流的雷鸟射猎，偶尔还能撞上一头熊。对了，我恍惚记得听谁说过，这一切的主人是、或是租给了一个英国人。……你们英国人有钱啊，你们瞧，没给挪威的穷人剩下多少。”

===Original===
I slept like a log on a bed quite as hard as a log, and woke to a brilliant blue morning, with the birds in the pinewoods fairly riotous, and snipe drumming in the boggy meadows, and the Skarso coming down like a sea. I could see the water almost up to the pathway of a long wooden bridge that led to the big farm Gaudian had spoken of. I got my glass on the torrent opposite, and saw the track to Snaasen winding up beside it till it was lost in a fold of the ravine. Above it I scanned the crown of the ridge, which was there much lower than on the sides of the fjord. There was no snow to be seen, and I knew by a sort of instinct that if I got up there I should find a broad tableland of squelching pastures with old snowdrifts in the hollows and tracts of scrubby dwarf birch.

===Chinese===
我睡得像根木头，那张床也硬得像根木头。醒来是一个明晃晃的蓝天早晨：松林里的鸟闹翻了天，沼泽草场上鹬鸟鼓翼嗡鸣，斯卡索河像大海一样奔泻而下。我看得见河水几乎漫到一座长木桥的桥面，那桥通往高迪安说的大农庄。我用望远镜对准对面的急流，看见通斯纳森的小径贴着水一路盘旋而上，最后消失在峡谷的一道褶皱里。我又细察上面山脊的顶——那里比峡湾两侧低得多。山上见不着雪，我凭一种本能就知道，我若上到那里，准会找到一片宽阔的高台湿草场，踩上去咕唧作响，洼地里积着旧雪堆，成片蓬乱的矮桦杂木。

===Original===
While I was waiting for breakfast I heard a noise from the high road, and saw a couple of the little conveyances they call *stolkjaeres* passing. My glass showed me Dr. Newhover in the first and a quantity of luggage in the second. They took the road across the wooden bridge to the big farm, and I could see the splash of their wheels at the far end of it, where the river was over the road. So Dr. Newhover, or some friend of his, was the lessee of this famous fishing, which carried with it the shooting on the uplands behind it. I rather thought I should spend the day finding out more about Snaasen, and I counted myself lucky to have got quarters in such an excellent observation-post as Peter Bojer’s cottage.

===Chinese===
等早饭的当口，我听见大路上有响动，看见两辆他们称作“斯托尔克雅雷”（*stolkjaeres*）的小马车驶过。望远镜里，第一辆坐着纽霍弗医生，第二辆装着大量行李。它们取道过了木桥往大农庄去，我看得见车轮在桥那头溅起水花——河水已经漫过了那段路。这么说，租下这著名钓场的，不是纽霍弗医生本人，便是他的某位朋友；这钓场还连带背后高地的射猎权。我想我不妨拿这一天工夫，把斯纳森的底细多摸出几分，也庆幸自己落脚在彼得·博耶尔家这样一个绝好的观察哨里。

===Original===
I wouldn’t go near the track to Snaasen till I saw what Newhover did, so Gaudian and I sat patiently at Peter Bojer’s window. About ten o’clock a couple of ponies laden with kit in charge of a towheaded boy appeared at the foot of the track and slowly climbed up the ravine. An hour later came Dr. Newhover, in a suit that looked like khaki and wearing a long mackintosh cape. He strode out well and breasted the steep path like a mountaineer. I wanted to go off myself in pursuit of him, keeping well behind, but Gaudian very sensibly pointed out how sparse the cover was, and that if he saw a man on that lonely road he would certainly want to know all about him.

===Chinese===
不看清纽霍弗的动向，我绝不肯靠近通斯纳森的小径，于是高迪安和我耐着性子守在彼得·博耶尔家的窗前。十点来钟，两匹驮着行装的矮马，由一个淡黄头发的男孩儿赶着，出现在小径脚下，慢慢爬上峡谷。一个钟头后，纽霍弗医生来了，穿一身近似卡其的衣裳，披一件长雨氅。他步子迈得漂亮，像个登山家似的迎着陡径而上。我自己也想随后追上去，远远缀着，可高迪安讲得很有道理：一路遮蔽太少，他若在那条僻静道上撞见一个人，必定要把人家查个底朝天。

===Original===
We sat out-of-doors after luncheon in a pleasant glare of sun, and by and by were rewarded by the sight of the pack-ponies returning, laden with a different size and shape of kit. They did not stop at the big farm, but crossed the wooden bridge and took the high road for Merdal. I concluded that this was the baggage of the man whom Newhover had replaced, and that he was returning to Stavanger in Kristian Egge’s boat. About teatime the man himself appeared﻿—Jason, or whatever his name was. I saw two figures come down the ravine by the Snaasen road, and stop at the foot and exchange farewells. One of them turned to go back, and I saw that this was Newhover, climbing with great strides like a man accustomed to hills. The other crossed the bridge, and passed within hail of us﻿—a foppish young man, my glass told me, wearing smart riding-breeches and with an aquascutum slung over his shoulder.

===Chinese===
午饭后我们坐在屋外，晒着一片舒坦的骄阳，不多时便得了报酬：那两匹驮马回来了，驮的家伙什大小形状都换了一副。它们没在大农庄停留，过了木桥，取大路往梅达尔去了。我断定这是被纽霍弗替下的那个人的行李，此人要搭克里斯蒂安·埃格的船回斯塔万格。喝下午茶的时分，那个人本人露面了——贾森，或是随便他姓什么。我看见两个人影顺着斯纳森那条路下了峡谷，在坡脚停住道别。一个转身往回走，我看清那是纽霍弗，正大步攀登，像个惯走山路的人。另一个过了桥，从我们喊一声就听得见的地方经过——望远镜告诉我，是个纨绔气派的年轻人，穿着时髦的马裤，肩上搭着一件“阿夸斯库图姆”（aquascutum）防雨大衣。

===Original===
I was very satisfied with what I had learned. I had seen Newhover relieve his predecessor, just as Medina had planned, and I knew where he was lodged. Whatever his secret was it was hidden in Snaasen, and to Snaasen I would presently go. Gaudian advised me to wait till after supper, when there would be light enough to find the way and not too much to betray us. So we both lay down and slept for four hours, and took the road about ten-thirty as fresh as yearlings.

===Chinese===
收获如此，我心满意足。我亲眼看见纽霍弗前来换他的前任，正如梅迪纳安排的那样，我也知道了他的落脚处。不管他的秘密是什么，总藏在斯纳森，而我马上就要去斯纳森。高迪安劝我等到晚饭之后，那时天光还够认路，又不至于亮得暴露我们。于是我们俩都躺下睡了四个钟头，十点半上路时，精神抖擞得像一岁口的幼驹。

===Original===
It was a noble night, windless and mild, and, though darkness lurked in the thickets and folds of hill, the sky was filled with a translucent amethyst glow. I felt as if I were out on some sporting expedition and enjoyed every moment of the walk with that strung-up expectant enjoyment which one gets in any form of chase. The torrent made wild music on our left hand, grumbling in pits and shooting over ledges with a sound like a snowslip. There was every kind of bird about, but I had to guess at them by their sounds and size, for there was no colour in that shadowy world.

===Chinese===
那是个庄严美好的夜，无风而和暖；灌木丛和山的褶皱里虽潜伏着黑暗，天上却盈满半透明的紫水晶光。我觉得自己像是出来赴一场什么游猎，一路每一个时刻都受用着那种绷紧了心弦、有所期待的快乐——无论追猎的是什么，人都尝得到这种滋味。急流在我们左手边奏着野性的音乐，在深潭里咕哝，掠过石坎时发出一声雪崩似的脆响。四下里什么样的鸟都有，但我只能凭声音和大小去猜它们，因为那个影影绰绰的世界里没有颜色。

===Original===
By and by we reached the top and had a light cold wind in our faces blowing from the snowy mountains to the north. The place seemed a huge broken tableland and every hollow glistened as if filled with snow or water. There were big dark shapes ahead of us which I took to be the hills beyond the Uradal. Here it was not so easy to follow the track, which twined about in order to avoid the boggy patches, and Gaudian and I frequently strayed from it and took tosses over snags of juniper. Once I was up against an iron pole, and to my surprise saw wires above. Gaudian nodded. “Snaasen is on the telephone,” he said.

===Chinese===
渐渐我们到了顶上，一股轻而冷的风迎面吹来，来自北方雪山。这地方像一大片破碎的高台地，每一处洼地都闪闪发亮，仿佛盛满了雪或水。前方有几大片黑影，我猜是乌拉达尔那头的山。在这里跟路便不那么容易了，小径为了绕开泥沼，扭来扭去，高迪安和我动不动就离了辙，让杜松的残桩绊得跌跌撞撞。有一回我撞上一根铁杆，吃惊地看见上头拉着电线。高迪安点头。“斯纳森通了电话，”他说。

===Original===
I had hoped to see some light in the house, so as to tell it from a distance. But we did not realise its presence till we were close upon it, standing a little back from the path, as dark as a tombstone. The inhabitants must have gone early to bed, for there was no sign of life within. It was a two-storeyed erection of wood, stoutly built, with broad eaves to the roof. Adjacent there stood a big barn or hayshed, and behind it some other outbuildings which might have been byres or dairies. We walked stealthily round the place, and were amazed at its utter stillness. There was no sound of an animal moving in the steading, and when a brace of mallards flew overhead we started at the noise like burglars at the creaking of a board.

===Chinese===
我本指望屋里能透出一点灯光，好远远辨认。可直到快撞上去，我们才意识到它就在眼前——从小径稍稍退后立着，黑得像块墓碑。住户想必睡得早，里头没有一丝活气。那是座两层的木建筑，造得结实，屋顶出檐宽大。旁边立着一座大谷仓或干草棚，后头还有几座杂用屋，或许是牛棚，或许是奶房。我们蹑手蹑脚绕着这地方走了一圈，对它那份彻底的静谧惊诧不已。场院里听不见一头牲口走动的声息；一对绿头鸭从头顶飞过，那点响动竟吓得我们一激灵，像夜贼听见地板吱呀一响。

===Original===
Short of burglary there was nothing further to be done, so we took the road home and scrambled at a great pace down the ravine, for it was chilly on the tableland. Before we went to bed, we had settled that next day Gaudian should go up to Snaasen like an ordinary tourist and make some excuse to get inside, while I would take a long tramp over the plateau, keeping well away from the house in case there might be something ado in that barren region.

===Chinese===
除了入室行窃，再没有别的可做，我们便取路回家，大步流星地连滚带爬冲下峡谷——高台地上冷得很。上床之前我们商定：第二天，高迪安装作寻常游客上斯纳森，寻个由头进屋；我则到高台上远走一遭，离那屋子远远的，以防那片荒瘠之地有什么动静。

===Original===
Next morning saw the same cloudless weather, and we started off about ten o’clock. I had a glorious but perfectly futile day. I went up the Skarso to well above the foss, and then climbed the north wall of the valley by a gulley choked with brushwood, which gave out long before the top and left me to finish my ascent by way of some very loose screes and unpleasant boiler-plates. I reached the plateau much farther to the east, where it was at a greater altitude, so that I looked down upon the depression where ran the track to Uradal. I struck due north among boggy meadows and the remains of old snowdrifts, through whose fringes flowers were showing, till I was almost on the edge of Uradal, and looked away beyond it to a fine cluster of rock peaks streaked and patched with ice. The Uradal glen was so deep cut that I could not see into it, so I moved west and struck the Merdal track well to the north of Snaasen. After that I fetched a circuit behind Snaasen, and had a good view of the house from the distance of about half a mile. Two of its chimneys were smoking, and there were sounds of farm work from the yard. There was no sign of live stock, but it looked as if someone was repairing the sheds against the summer season. I waited for more than an hour, but I saw no human being, so I turned homeward, and made a careful descent by the ravine, reconnoitring every corner in case I should run into Newhover.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨还是那样万里无云的天，我们十点来钟动身。我过了风光无限却全然徒劳的一天。我沿斯卡索河上到瀑布以上很远，然后从一条灌丛壅塞的沟壑攀登谷地的北壁；那沟壑离顶还早早就到了头，害我改走一段非常松动的碎石坡和几片活像锅炉铁板的滑石板，才算登了顶。我到达高台地的位置偏东得多，那里地势更高，所以我俯瞰着那道洼地——通往乌拉达尔的小径就从那里过。我朝正北行进，穿行在沼泽草场和旧雪堆的残迹之间，雪堆边上已探出花来，一直走到几乎到了乌拉达尔的边沿，望见它远处一簇峻峭的岩峰，冰痕斑驳。乌拉达尔峡谷切得太深，我看不进去，便折向西，在斯纳森以北相当远的地方撞上了回梅达尔的小径。此后我绕到斯纳森背后兜了一圈，在约半英里外把那座屋子看了个真切。两根烟囱冒着烟，场院里传来农活的声响。看不到牲口的影子，倒像是有谁在修缮棚舍，预备夏季的到来。我守了一个多钟头，没见一个人影，便转身回程，顺着峡谷小心地下来，每个拐角都先侦察一番，生怕迎面撞上纽霍弗。

===Original===
I found that Gaudian had returned before me. When I asked him what luck he had had he shook his head.

===Chinese===
我发现高迪安已先我回来。我问他运气如何，他摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I played the part of a weary traveller, and asked for milk. An ugly woman gave me beer. She said she had no milk, till the cattle came up from the valleys. She would not talk and she was deaf. She said an English Herr had the ryper shooting, but lived at Tryssil. That is the name of the big farm by the Skarso. She would tell me no more, and I saw no other person. But I observed that Snaasen is larger than I thought. There are rooms built out at the back, which we thought were barns. There is ample space there for a man to be concealed.”

===Chinese===
“我扮了一个疲乏的旅人，讨牛奶喝。一个丑女人给了我啤酒。她说眼下没有奶，要等牛群从下面的谷地赶上来。她不肯说话，耳朵还背。她说雷鸟射猎是一位英国老爷租的，可那位老爷住在特吕西尔（Tryssil）。那就是斯卡索河边那座大农庄的名字。她再不肯多讲一句，旁的人我一个也没见着。不过我注意到，斯纳森比我原先想的大。后头接出了几间屋子，我们原以为是谷仓的。那里面的地方，藏下一个人绰绰有余。”

===Original===
I asked him if he had any plan, and he said he thought of going boldly up next day and asking for Newhover, whom he could say he had seen passing Peter Bojer’s cottage. He disliked the man, but had never openly quarrelled with him. I approved of that, but in the meantime I resolved to do something on my own account that night. I was getting anxious, for I felt that my time was growing desperately short; it was now the 25th of April and I was due back in London on the 29th, and, if I failed to turn up, Medina would make inquiries at Fosse Manor and suspect. I had made up my mind to go alone that night to Snaasen and do a little pacific burgling.

===Chinese===
我问他可有什么打算，他说他想第二天索性大大方方上去，指名找纽霍弗——就说看见他从彼得·博耶尔家门前经过好了。他厌恶此人，却从没同他公开闹翻。我赞成这个主意；但与此同时，我打定主意当晚自己去干点什么。我渐渐着急起来，只觉得手头的时间紧得要命：这天已是4月25日，29日我就该回伦敦；我若不露面，梅迪纳就会到福斯庄园去打听，起疑心。我已拿定主意，当夜独自上斯纳森，干一回小小的、不伤人的夜窃。

===Original===
I set out about eleven, and I put my pistol in my pocket, as well as my flask and sandwiches and electric torch, for it occurred to me that anything might happen. I made good going across the bridge and up the first part of the track, for I wanted to have as much time as possible for my job. My haste was nearly my undoing, for instead of reconnoitring and keeping my ears open, I strode up the hill as if I had been walking to make a record. It was by the mercy of Heaven that I was at a point where an outjutting boulder made a sharp corner when I was suddenly aware that someone was coming down the road. I flattened myself into the shadow, and saw Newhover.

===Chinese===
十一点来钟我出发了。我把手枪揣进衣袋，还带了酒壶、三明治和电筒，因为我想到了，什么事都可能发生。过桥和上小径头一段我走得飞快，因为我想给正事留出尽可能多的工夫。这份急躁险些坏我的事：我没有边走边侦察、竖起耳朵听，倒像要去破纪录似的，迈开大步往山上冲。全亏上天怜悯，我恰好走到一处凸出的巨岩形成的急拐角，才猛然察觉有人正顺路下来。我把身子贴进阴影，看见了纽霍弗。

===Original===
He did not see or hear me, for he, too, was preoccupied. He was descending at a good pace, and he must have started in a hurry, for he had no hat. His longish blond locks were all tousled, and his face seemed sharper than usual with anxiety.

===Chinese===
他没看见我，也没听见我，因为他自己也是一副心事重重的样子。他下山的步子不慢，动身时必定匆忙，连帽子都没戴。他那头淡金色的长发乱作一团，脸孔叫忧灼磨得比平时尖削。

===Original===
I wondered what on earth had happened, and my first notion was to follow him downhill. And then it occurred to me that his absence gave me a sovereign chance at Snaasen. But if the household was astir there might be other travellers on the road and it behoved me to go warily. Now, near the top of the ravine, just under the edge of the tableland, there was a considerable patch of wood﻿—birches, juniper, and windblown pines﻿—for there the torrent flowed in a kind of cup, after tumbling off the plateau and before hurling itself down to the valley. Here it was possible to find an alternative road to the path, so I dived in among the matted whortleberries and moss-covered boulders.

===Chinese===
我纳闷到底出了什么事，第一个念头是跟着他下山。随即又想到，他这一走，给了我在斯纳森一个万难再得的机会。可若是屋里人已经起身，路上兴许还有别的行人，我必须走得谨慎。恰好在峡谷近顶、高台地边缘的紧下方，有一大片树林——桦树、杜松，还有被风掀歪的松树——因为急流翻下高台之后、扑向谷底之前，在那里淌成一个杯状的潭。在这里有可能找出一条小径之外的便道，我便一头钻进了纠结的越橘丛和生满苔藓的巨石之间。

===Original===
I had not gone ten yards before I realised that there was somebody or something else in the thicket. There was a sound of plunging ahead of me, then the crack of a rotten log, then the noise of a falling stone. It might be a beast, but it struck me that no wild thing would move so awkwardly. Only human boots make that kind of clumsy slipping.

===Chinese===
走不出十码我就发觉，林子里还有别的什么人，或者什么东西。前头传来一阵扑腾声，接着是一声朽木的脆裂，再是一块石头滚落的动静。或许是头野兽，但我转念一想，没有哪个野物会行动得这样笨拙。只有人的靴子，才会踩出那样憨拙的打滑声。

===Original===
If this was somebody from Snaasen, what was he doing off the track? Could he be watching me? Well, I proposed to do a little stalking on my own account. I got down on all-fours and crawled in cover in the direction of the sound. It was very dark there, but I could see a faint light where the scrub thinned round the stream.

===Chinese===
若这是斯纳森来的人，他离了小径在做什么？莫非他在监视我？好啊，那我倒要反过来潜近他一回。我趴下四肢，借掩体朝声音的方向爬去。那里黑得很，但在灌木稀疏、临水的地段，我望得见一点微光。

===Original===
Soon I was at the edge of the yeasty water. The sounds had stopped, but suddenly they began again a little farther up, and there was a scuffle as if part of the bank had given way. The man, whoever he was, seemed to be trying to cross. That would be a dangerous thing to do, for the torrent was wide and very strong. I crawled a yard or two upstream, and then in an open patch saw what was happening.

===Chinese===
很快我到了翻着白沫的水边。那声响停了，可忽然又在上游一点的地方响起，还夹着一阵窸窣挣扎，像是有一段岸塌了。那个人，不管他是谁，像是在想法子过河。这可是玩命的举动，因为急流又宽又猛。我向上游爬了一两码，随即在一小块开阔地里看清了眼前的情形。

===Original===
A fallen pine made a crazy bridge to a great rock, from which the rest of the current might conceivably be leaped. A man was kneeling on the trunk and beginning to move along it.﻿ ﻿… But as I looked the rotten thing gave way and the next I saw he was struggling in the foam. It was all the matter of a fraction of a second, and before I knew I was leaning over the brink and clutching at an arm. I gripped it, braced one leg against a rock, and hauled the owner close into the edge out of the main current. He seemed to have taken no hurt, for he found a foothold, and scarcely needed my help to scramble up beside me.

===Chinese===
一棵倒下的松树，歪歪斜斜搭成一座通向一块巨石的险桥，从那石头上，剩下的那段水流兴许还能一跃而过。一个人正跪在树干上，开始往前挪。……可就在我望着的时候，那截朽木断了，转眼间我看见他已在浪沫里挣扎。这一切不过一眨眼的工夫，我还没回过神，已经探身在水沿上，一把攥住一条手臂。我抓牢它，一条腿抵住岩石，把那手臂的主人拽到岸边激流的边上。他似乎没有受伤，因为他自己找到了落脚点，几乎用不着我帮，就爬上来到了我身边。

===Original===
Then to my surprise he went for me tooth and nail. It was like the assault of a wild beast, and its suddenness rolled me on my back. I felt hands on my throat, and grew angry, caught the wrists and wrenched them away. I flung a leg over his back and got uppermost, and after that he was at my mercy. He seemed to realise it, too, for he lay quite quiet and did not struggle.

===Chinese===
谁知叫我吃惊的是，他劈头盖脸朝我扑来。那简直像野兽的突袭，那份猝不及防把我掀了个仰面朝天。我觉出两只手掐上我的喉咙，火了起来，攥住那对手腕，狠狠拧开。我一腿甩过他的背，压到了上风，从此他便任我发落。他似乎也明白这一点，因为他安安静静躺着，不再挣扎。

===Original===
“What the devil do you mean?” I said angrily. “You’d have been drowned but for me, and then you try to throttle me.”

===Chinese===
“你到底是什么意思？”我怒道。“没有我你早淹死了，回头倒想掐死我。”

===Original===
I got out my torch and had a look at him. It was the figure of a slight young man, dressed in rough homespun such as Norwegian farm lads wear. His face was sallow and pinched, and decorated with the most preposterous wispish beard, and his hair was cut roughly as if with garden shears. The eyes that looked up at me were as scared and wild as a deer’s.

===Chinese===
我掏出电筒照他。那是个瘦弱的年轻人的身形，穿着挪威农家小伙子常穿的那种粗呢土布。他脸色蜡黄、面颊凹陷，下巴上装点着一部荒唐透顶的稀疏胡子，头发剪得七长八短，像用园艺剪铰出来的。那双仰望着我的眼睛，惊惶而狂乱，像一头鹿的。

===Original===
“What the devil do you mean?” I repeated, and then to my surprise he replied in English.

===Chinese===
“你到底是什么意思？”我又问了一句。这时他竟用英语答了话，叫我吃了一惊。

===Original===
“Let me up,” he said, “I’m too tired to fight. I’ll go back with you.”

===Chinese===
“放我起来，”他说，“我累得打不动了。我跟你回去。”

===Original===
Light broke in on me.

===Chinese===
我心里豁然一亮。

===Original===
“Don’t you worry, old chap,” I said soothingly. “You’re going back with me, but not to that infernal saeter. We’ve met before, you know. You’re Lord Mercot, and I saw you ride ‘Red Prince’ last year at the ‘House’ Grind.”

===Chinese===
“别怕，老弟，”我温声说。“你是要跟我回去，可不是回那座该死的山间牧场。我们从前见过面，你是知道的。你是默科特勋爵，去年在“豪斯”逐猎会（the 'House' Grind）上，我看见你骑“红王子”（Red Prince）来着。”

===Original===
He was sitting up, staring at me like a ghost.

===Chinese===
他坐起身，像见了鬼似的直瞪着我。

===Original===
“Who are you? Oh, for God’s sake, who are you?”

===Chinese===
“你是谁？哦，看在上帝分上，你到底是谁？”

===Original===
“Hannay’s my name. I live at Fosse Manor in the Cotswolds. You once came to dine with us before the Heythrop Ball.”

===Chinese===
“我叫汉内。住在科茨沃尔德的福斯庄园。海索普舞会（Heythrop Ball）之前，你还来我们家吃过一回晚饭。”

===Original===
“Hannay!” He repeated stumblingly﻿—“I remember﻿—I think﻿—remember﻿—remember Lady Hannay. Yes﻿—and Fosse. It’s on the road between﻿—”

===Chinese===
“汉内！”他磕磕绊绊地念着——“我记得——我想是——记得——记得汉内夫人。对了——还有福斯。就在那条路当中，介于——”

===Original===
He scrambled to his feet.

===Chinese===
他手忙脚乱地爬起来。

===Original===
“Oh, sir, get me away. He’s after me﻿—the new devil with the long face, the man who first brought me here. I don’t know what has happened to me, but I’ve been mad a long time, and I’ve only got sane in the last days. Then I remembered﻿—and I ran away. But they’re after me. Oh, quick, quick! Let’s hide.”

===Chinese===
“哦，先生，带我走。他在追我——那个长脸的新魔鬼，头一回把我带到这儿来的那个人。我不知道自己出了什么事，可我疯了很久，直到最近这几天才清醒过来。然后我想起来了——就逃了出来。可他们在追我。哦，快，快！咱们躲起来。”

===Original===
“See here, my lad,” I said, and I took out my pistol. “The first man that lays a hand on you I shoot, and I don’t miss. You’re as safe now as if you were at home. But this is no place to talk, and I’ve the devil of a lot to tell you. I’m going to take you down with me to my lodging in the valley. But they’re hunting you, so we’ve got to go cannily. Are you fit to walk? Well, do exactly as I tell you, and in an hour you’ll be having a long drink and looking up timetables.”

===Chinese===
“听着，小伙子，”我说，一面掏出手枪。“谁敢先碰你一根指头，我就开枪，而且我不打偏。这会儿你跟在家里一样安全。可这地方不是说话的所在，我有一大车话要讲给你。我这就带你下到谷里我的住处。不过他们在猎你，咱们得放机灵些。你还走得动吗？好，一切照我说的做，不出一个钟头，你就能痛痛快快喝上一大通，翻着时刻表查车次了。”

===Original===
I consider that journey back a creditable piece of piloting. The poor boy was underfed and shaking with excitement, but he stepped out gallantly, and obeyed me like a lamb. We kept off the track so as to muffle our steps in grass, and took every corner like scouts in a reconnaissance. We met Newhover coming back, but we heard him a long way off and were in good cover when he passed. He was hurrying as furiously as ever and I could hear his laboured breathing. After that we had a safe road over the meadow, but we crossed the bridge most circumspectly, making sure that there was no one in the landscape. About half-past one I pushed open Gaudian’s bedroom window, woke him, and begged him to forage for food and drink.

===Chinese===
我认为，回程这一趟领路的功夫相当拿得出手。这可怜的孩子营养不足，又激动得直打颤，但他勇敢地迈着步子，像只羔羊一样听我调遣。我们避开小径，好让脚步声闷进草里，每过一个拐角都像侦察兵摸哨一般。半路我们迎面遇上了归来的纽霍弗，但隔老远就听见了他，等他经过时我们已藏得妥妥帖帖。他还跟先前一样发疯似的赶路，我听得见他费力的喘息。这以后，穿草场的路便太平了，但过桥时我们百倍小心，先看清四野确无一人。一点半左右，我推开高迪安卧室的窗子，唤醒他，求他去张罗些吃的喝的。

===Original===
“Did you get into Snaasen?” he asked sleepily.

===Chinese===
“你进斯纳森了吗？”他睡意朦胧地问。

===Original===
“No, but I’ve found what we’ve been looking for. One of the three hostages is at this moment sitting on your cabin-box.”

===Chinese===
“没有，可我找到了我们一直找的东西。三名人质里的一位，这会儿正坐在你的衣箱上呢。”

## XXI How I Stalked Wilder Game Than Deer / 二十一、我猎逐比鹿更野的猎物

## I 9 a.m. to 2:15 p.m. / 一、上午九时至下午二时一刻

===Original===
Obviously I could make no plan, and I had no clear idea in my head as to what kind of settlement I wanted with Medina. I was certain that I should find him somewhere on the hill, and that, if he got a chance, he would try to kill me. The odds were, of course, against his succeeding straight off, but escape was not what I sought﻿—I must get rid of this menace forever. I don’t think that I wanted to kill him, but indeed I never tried to analyse my feelings. I was obeying a blind instinct, and letting myself drift on the tides of fate.

===Chinese===
显然，我定不出任何计划，脑子里也不清楚，自己到底想同梅迪纳做个什么样的了断。我只确定一件事：他就在山上某处，而只要得了机会，他就会想法要我的命。当然，他未必一上来就得手，可我要的并不是脱身——我必须把这威胁永远除掉。我想我并不想杀他，但说实话，我也从没想过去剖析自己的心绪。我只是听凭一股盲目的本能驱使，任凭自己随命运的潮水漂荡。

===Original===
Corrie-na-Sidhe is an upper corrie, separated from the Aicill valley by a curtain of rock and scree which I dare say was once the moraine of a glacier and down which the Alt-na-Sidhe tumbled in a fine chain of cascades. So steep is its fall that no fish can ascend it, so that, while at the foot it is full of sizable trout, in the Corrie itself it holds nothing, as Greenslade reported, but little dark fingerlings. It was very warm as we mounted the chaos of slabs and boulders, where a very sketchy and winding track had been cut for bringing down the deer. Only the toughest kind of pony could make that ascent. Though the day was young the heat was already great, and the glen behind us swam in a glassy sheen. Kennedy, as usual, mopped his brow and grunted, but the lean Angus strode ahead as if he were on the flat.

===Chinese===
科里纳希（Corrie-na-Sidhe）是一处高处的圈谷，与艾基尔河谷之间隔着一道岩壁与碎石的帘幕，我敢说那原是某条冰川的冰碛，阿尔特纳希溪（Alt-na-Sidhe）便顺着它泻下一串漂亮的瀑布。落差陡得鱼根本游不上去，于是山脚下满是像样的鳟鱼，而据格林斯莱德说，圈谷里头除了些黑黢黢的小鱼苗，什么也没有。我们往上攀的那片乱石坂与巨石堆里热得很，其间开出了一条草率的之字形小道，专供往山下拖鹿，只有最结实的那类矮种马才上得去。天色尚早，暑气已经逼人，身后的峡谷浸在一层玻璃似的亮光里。肯尼迪照旧抹着额头咕哝，精瘦的安格斯却大步抢在前头，仿佛走的是平地。

===Original===
At the edge of the corrie we halted for a spy. Deep hollows have a trick of drawing the wind, and such faint currents of air as I could detect seemed to be coming on our left rear from the northeast. Angus was positive, however, that though the south had gone out of the wind, it was pretty well due east, with no north in it, and maintained that when we were farther up the corrie we would have it fair on our left cheek. We were not long in finding beasts. There was a big drove of hinds on the right bank of the burn, and another lot, with a few small stags, on the left bank, well up on the face of Bheinn Fhada. But there was nothing shootable there.

===Chinese===
到了圈谷边沿，我们停下来瞭望。深谷洼地有个拢风的脾性，我所能辨出的些微气流，似乎是从东北方吹在我们左后方。可安格斯一口咬定，风里固然没了南的成分，却差不多是正东，不带一丝北意，还说等我们再往圈谷高处走些，风就会正正吹在我们左脸颊上。没多久我们就找到了兽群。溪流右岸有一大群母鹿，左岸又有一群，带着几只小公鹿，高高地在比恩法达（Bheinn Fhada）的山坡上。只是那里没有值得开枪的东西。

===Original===
“The big stags will be all on the high tops,” said Angus. “We must be getting up to the burnhead.”

===Chinese===
“大公鹿全在高顶上，”安格斯说，“我们得往溪头上去。”

===Original===
It was easier said than done, for there were the hinds to be circumvented, so we had to make a long circuit far up the hill called Clonlet, which is the westernmost of the Machray tops south of the Aicill. It was rough going, for we mounted to about the 3,000 feet level, and traversed the hillside just under the upper scarp of rock. Presently we were looking down upon the cup which was the head of the corrie, and over the col could see the peak of Stob Coire Easain and the ridge of Stob Bán, both on Haripol and beyond the Reascuill. We had another spy, and made out two small lots of stags on the other side of the Alt-na-Sidhe. They were too far off to get a proper view of them, but one or two looked good beasts, and I decided to get nearer.

===Chinese===
这话说来容易做来难，因为得绕开那些母鹿，于是我们只好远远兜个大圈子，上了名叫克朗利特（Clonlet）的山——那是艾基尔以南马赫雷诸峰中最西的一座。一路难走，我们爬到大约 3,000 英尺的高度，贴着上段岩壁的下方横切山坡。不多时，我们已俯瞰到圈谷头部那只“杯”，越过山坳还能望见斯托布科里伊森（Stob Coire Easain）的峰顶和斯托布班（Stob Bán）的山脊，两者都在哈里波尔境内、雷亚斯库尔河（the Reascuill）的彼岸。我们又瞭望了一番，认出阿尔特纳希溪对岸有两小群公鹿。距离太远看不真切，但有一两头像是好兽，我便决定凑近些。

===Original===
We had to make a cautious descent of the hillside in case of deer lying in pockets, for the place was seamed with gullies. Before we were halfway down I got my telescope on one of the lots, and picked out a big stag with a poor head, which clearly wanted shooting. Angus agreed, and we started down a sheltering ravine to get to the burnside. The sight of a quarry made me forget everything else, and for the next hour and a half I hadn’t a thought in the world except how to get within range of that beast. One stalk is very much like another, and I am not going to describe this. The only trouble came from a small stag in our rear, which had come over Clonlet and got the scent of our track on the hill-face. This unsettled him and he went off at a great pace towards the top of the burn. I thought at first that the brute would go up Bheinn Fhada and carry off our lot with him, but he came to a halt, changed his mind, and made for the Haripol march and the col.

===Chinese===
我们只得小心翼翼地下山坡，提防有鹿卧在洼地里，因为这地方沟壑纵横。还没下到半坡，我的望远镜就锁定其中一群，挑出一头大公鹿，角相甚劣，明摆着该打。安格斯同意，我们便顺着一条有遮蔽的沟壑往溪边下去。一见了猎物，我便把其余一切忘了个干净，此后一个半钟头里，满脑子只剩一件事：怎样进入那畜生的射程。一次潜猎同另一次大同小异，我不打算细讲。唯一的麻烦来自我们背后的一只小公鹿——它翻过克朗利特，在山坡上嗅到了我们留下的踪迹。这一惊，它便撒开腿朝溪头飞奔而去。我起初还怕这畜生会蹿同比恩法达，把我们要的那群也一并卷走，谁知它跑到半路收住脚，改了主意，直奔哈里波尔的地界和山坳去了。

===Original===
After that it was plain sailing. We crawled up the right of the Alt-na-Sidhe, which was first-class cover, and then turned up a tributary gully which came down from Bheinn Fhada. Indeed the whole business was too simple to be of much interest to anyone, except the man with the rifle. When I judged I was about the latitude of my stag, I crept out of the burn and reached a hillock from which I had a good view of him. The head, as I suspected, was poor﻿—only nine points, though the horns were of the rough, thick, old Highland type, but the body was heavy, and he was clearly a back-going beast. After a wait of some twenty minutes he got up and gave me a chance at about two hundred yards, and I dropped him dead with a shot in the neck, which was the only part of him clear.

===Chinese===
此后便一帆风顺。我们沿着阿尔特纳希溪右岸爬行——那是头等的掩蔽——随后拐进一条从比恩法达泻下的支沟。说实在的，整桩事简单得除了端枪的人，谁也不会觉得有多大意思。估摸着到了同那头公鹿大致齐平的地方，我爬出溪床，摸上一座小丘，从那儿能把它看个清楚。角相果如我所疑——只有九叉，虽说角身是那种粗厚的高地老式样，可它躯体沉重，明明白白是头过了盛年、走下坡路的老兽。等了约莫二十分钟，它站起身来，在约两百码外给了我一个机会，我一枪打中它的颈子，把它放倒在地——那是它全身唯一露出来的部分。

===Original===
It was for me the first stag of the season, and it is always a pleasant moment when the tension relaxes and you light your pipe and look around you. As soon as the gralloch was over I proposed lunch, and we found for the purpose a little nook by a spring. We were within a few hundred yards of the Haripol march, which there does not run along the watershed but crosses the corrie about half a mile below the col. In the old days of sheep there had been a fence, the decaying posts of which could be observed a little way off on a knoll. Between the fence and the col lay some very rough ground, where the Alt-na-Sidhe had its source, ground so broken that it was impossible, without going a good way up the hill, to see from it the watershed ridge.

===Chinese===
这是我本季的头一头公鹿，而紧张的弦一松、点上烟斗四下张望，这样的时刻总是惬意的。猎物一开膛收拾停当，我便提议吃午饭，我们在一处泉边觅得一个小小的角落。此地离哈里波尔的地界只有几百码，地界在这一段并不顺着分水岭走，而是在山坳下方约半英里处横穿圈谷。牧羊的旧年代里这里有过一道栅栏，朽坏的桩子还可在不远处的一座小丘上望见。栅栏与山坳之间横着一片极其崎岖的地带，阿尔特纳希溪便发源其间——那地面破破碎碎，除非往山上走出老远，从那里根本望不见分水岭的山脊。

===Original===
I finished Mary’s stuffed scones and ginger biscuits, and had a drink of whisky and spring water, while Angus and Kennedy ate their lunch a few yards off in the heather. I was just lighting my pipe, when a sound made me pause with the match in my hand. A rifle bullet sang over my head. It was not very near﻿—fifty feet or so above me, and a little to the left.

===Chinese===
我吃完了玛丽做的夹心烤饼和姜汁饼干，就着泉水喝了口威士忌，安格斯和肯尼迪则在几码外的石楠丛里用他们的午饭。我正要点烟斗，一声响动使我捏着火柴停在了半空。一颗步枪子弹嗖地掠过我头顶。离得不很近——在我上方五十来英尺，略偏左。

===Original===
“The tamned towrists!” I heard Angus exclaim.

===Chinese===
“这帮该死的游客！”我听见安格斯嚷道。

===Original===
I knew it was Medina as certainly as if I had seen him. He was somewhere in the rough ground between the Haripol march and the col﻿—probably close to the col, for the sound of the report seemed to come from a good way off. He could not have been aiming at me, for I was perfectly covered, but he must have seen me when I stalked the stag. He had decided that his chance was not yet come, and the shot was camouflage﻿—to keep up the reputation of Haripol for wild shooting.

===Chinese===
我知道那是梅迪纳，就像亲眼看见了他一样确凿。他就在哈里波尔地界与山坳之间的那片乱石地里——多半紧挨着山坳，因为枪声听着是从老远的地方传来的。他不可能在瞄我，我掩蔽得严严实实，但他想必是在我潜猎公鹿时瞧见了我。他断定自己的时机未到，这一枪不过是障眼法——好保住哈里波尔那枪法狂野的名声。

===Original===
“It would be the staggie that went over the march,” grunted Angus. “The towrists﻿—to be shooting at such a wee beast!”

===Chinese===
“八成是那只蹿过地界的小公鹿，”安格斯咕哝道，“这帮游客——竟朝这么个小小的畜生开枪！”

===Original===
I had suddenly made up my mind. I would give Medina the opportunity he sought. I would go and look for him.

===Chinese===
我突然拿定了主意。我要把梅迪纳寻求的机会给他。我要主动去找他。

===Original===
I got up and stretched my legs. “I’m going to try a stalk on my own,” I told Angus. “I’ll go over to Corrie Easain. You had better pull this beast down to the burnside, and then fetch the pony. You might send Hughie and the other pony up Glenaicill to the Mad Burn. If I get a stag I’ll gralloch him and get him down somehow to the burn, so tell Hughie to look out for my signal. I’ll wave a white handkerchief. The wind is backing round to the north, Angus. It should be all right for Corrie Easain, if I take it from the south.”

===Chinese===
我站起身，伸了伸腿。“我要自己单独潜猎一回，”我告诉安格斯，“我到伊森圈谷（Corrie Easain）那边去。你最好把这畜生拖到溪边，然后去牵马。你可以打发休伊带着另一匹矮种马，沿格伦艾基尔上到疯溪（the Mad Burn）那儿。我若打着了公鹿，会开膛收拾，再想法子把它弄下溪边，所以叫休伊留意我的信号。我会挥一条白手绢。风在转向北面，安格斯。只要我从南边接近，去伊森圈谷风向应该正合适。”

===Original===
